This is useful for\n40\t    computing scattering and absorption cross-sections without needing additional\n41\t    normalization.\n42\t\n43\t    Key characteristics:\n44\t    - The source injects 1 W of power per um^2 of source area along the injection_axis\n45\t    - The normalization for the incident field is |E_0|^2 = 2/(c*eps0) for any source size\n46\t    - For angled incidence, the same power is injected along the injection_axis, not\n47\t      the propagation direction\n48\t    - The source region is a 3D volume with finite extents in all three dimensions\n49\t\n50\t    The TFSF boundary interaction works as follows:\n51\t    - Fields inside the TFSF box contain the total field (incident + scattered)\n52\t    - Fields outside the TFSF box contain only the scattered field\n53\t    - The incident field is cancelled at the TFSF boundary edges\n54\t\n55\t    Phase 1 supports TFSF with:\n56\t    - Normal incidence (angle_theta=0)\n57\t    - Oblique incidence with angle_theta and angle_phi\n58\t    - Chebyshev broadband method (num_freqs >= 1)\n59\t\n60\t    Example\n61\t    -------\n62\t    >>> from autofdtd import GaussianPulse, TFSF\n63\t    >>> pulse = GaussianPulse(freq0=200e12, fwidth=20e12)\n64\t    >>> tfsf = TFSF(\n65\t    ...     center=(0, 0, 0),\n66\t    ...     size=(2e-6, 2e-6, 2e-6),\n67\t    ...     source_time=pulse,\n68\t    ...     injection_axis=2,  # z-axis\n69\t    ...     direction=\"+\",\n70\t    ...     pol_angle=0.0,\n71\t    ... )\n72\t    \"\"\"\n73\t\n74\t    type: Literal[\"TFSF\"] = \"TFSF\"\n75\t    center: Vec3 = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0)\n76\t    size: Vec3\n77\t    source_time: GaussianPulse | ContinuousWave | BroadbandPulse | CustomSourceTime\n78\t    injection_axis: int = Field(\n79\t        title=\"Injection Axis\",\n80\t        description=\"Specifies the injection axis. CustomSourceTime,\n    SourceTimeModel,\n    source_time_model_from_value,\n)\n\n\ndef _normalize_name(name: str | None) -> str | None:\n    if name is None:\n        return None\n    stripped = \"-\"]\n\n\nclass TFSF(TaggedModel):\n    \"\"\"Total-field scattered-field (TFSF) source that injects a plane wave in a finite region.\n\n    The TFSF source separates the simulation domain into total-field (inside the source\n    region) and scattered-field (outside the source region) regions. This is useful for\n    computing scattering and absorption cross-sections without needing additional\n    normalization.\n\n    Key characteristics:\n    - The source injects 1 W of power per um^2 of source area along the injection_axis\n    - The normalization for the incident field is |E_0|^2 = 2/(c*eps0) for any source size\n    - For angled incidence, the same power is injected along the injection_axis, not\n      the propagation direction\n    - The source region is a 3D volume with finite extents in all three dimensions\n\n    The TFSF boundary interaction works as follows:\n    - Fields inside the TFSF box contain the total field (incident + scattered)\n    - Fields outside the TFSF box contain only the scattered field\n    - The incident field is cancelled at the TFSF boundary edges\n\n    Phase 1 supports TFSF with:\n    - Normal incidence (angle_theta=0)\n    - Oblique incidence with angle_theta and angle_phi\n    - Chebyshev broadband method (num_freqs >= 1)\n\n    Example\n    -------\n    >>> from autofdtd import GaussianPulse, TFSF\n    >>> pulse = GaussianPulse(freq0=200e12, fwidth=20e12)\n    >>> tfsf = TFSF(\n    ...     center=(0, 0, 0),\n    ...     size=(2e-6, 2e-6, 2e-6),\n    ...     source_time=pulse,\n    ...     injection_axis=2,  # z-axis\n    ...     direction=\"+\",\n    ...     pol_angle=0.0,\n    ... )\n    \"\"\"\n\n    type: Literal[\"TFSF\"] = \"TFSF\"\n    center: Vec3 = (0.0, 0.0, 0.0)\n    size: Vec3\n    source_time: GaussianPulse | ContinuousWave | BroadbandPulse | CustomSourceTime\n    injection_axis: int = Field(\n        title=\"Injection Axis\",\n        description=\"Specifies the injection axis. The plane of incidence is defined via this \"\n        \"injection_axis and the direction. The propagation axis is defined with respect \"\n        \"to the injection_axis by angle_theta and angle_phi.\",\n    )\n    direction: Direction = Field(\n        default=\"+\",\n        title=\"Direction\",\n        description=\"Specifies propagation in the positive or negative direction of the injection \"\n        \"axis.\",\n    )\n    angle_theta: float = Field(\n        default=0.0,\n        title=\"Polar Angle\",\n        description=\"Polar angle of the propagation axis from the injection axis.\",\n    )\n    angle_phi: float = Field(\n        default=0.0,\n        title=\"Azimuth Angle\",\n        description=\"Azimuth angle of the propagation axis in the plane orthogonal to the \"\n        \"injection axis.\",\n    )\n    pol_angle: float = Field(\n        default=0.0,\n        title=\"Polarization Angle\",\n        description=\"Specifies the angle between the electric field polarization of the \"\n        \"source and the plane defined by the injection axis and the propagation axis (rad). \"\n        \"pol_angle=0 (default) specifies P polarization, \"\n        \"while pol_angle=pi/2 specifies S polarization.\",\n    )\n    num_freqs: int = Field(\n        default=1,\n        title=\"Number of Frequency Points\",\n        description=\"Number of points used to approximate the frequency dependence of the injected \"\n        \"field.    if value not in (0, 1, 2):\n            raise ValueError(\"injection_axis must be 0 (x), 1 (y), or 2 (z)\")\n        return value\n\n    @field_validator(\"center\")\n    @classmethod\n    def _validate_center(cls, value: Sequence[object]) -> Vec3:\n        if len(value) != 3:\n            raise ValueError(\"center must contain exactly three components\")\n        normalized = (float(value[0]), float(value[1]), float(value[2]))\n        if any(not math.isfinite(component) for component in normalized):\n            raise ValueError(\"center components must be finite\")\n        return normalized\n\n    @field_validator(\"size\")\n    @classmethod\n    def _validate_size(cls, value: Sequence[object]) -> Vec3:\n        if len(value) != 3:\n            raise ValueError(\"size must contain exactly three components\")\n        normalized = tuple(float(v) for v in value)\n        if any(not math.isfinite(component) for component in normalized):\n            raise ValueError(\"size components must be finite\")\n        # TFSF is a volume source - all dimensions should be non-zero\n        if any(math.isclose(s, 0.0) for s in normalized):\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"TFSF requires a 3D volume (all size components must be non-zero), \"\n                f\"got size={normalized}\"\n            )\n        return normalized\n\n \"\"\"Source polarization normal vector in cartesian coordinates.\n\n        Returns a unit vector pointing in the electric field polarization direction.\n        \"\"\"\n        # Polarization vector original injection axis\n        return self._unpop_axis(pol_z_final, (pol_x_final, pol_y_final))\n\n    @property\n    def _reference_wavelength(self) -> float | None:\n        \"\"\"Reference wavelength from source_time if available.\"\"\"\n        if isinstance(self.source_time, GaussianPulse):\n            freq0 = getattr(self.source_time, \"freq0\", None)\n            if freq0 and freq0 > 0:\n                return 2.998e8 / freq0\n        return None\n\n    def _unpop_axis(self, z_component: float, xy_pair: tuple[float, float]) -> Vec3:\n        \"\"\"Restore components to the original injection axis ordering.\"\"\"\n        axis = self.injection_axis\n        result = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0]\n        if axis == 0:\n            result[0] = z_component\n            result[1] = xy_pair[0]\n            result[2] = xy_pair[1]\n        elif axis == 1:\n            result[0] = xy_pair[0]\n            result[1] = z_component\n            result[2] = xy_pair[1]\n        else:  # compiled_source.has_magnetic and compiled_source.h_field_data is not None:\n266\t        for placement, weight in zip(\n267\t            compiled_source.placements,\n268\t            compiled_source.placement_weights,\n269\t            strict=True,\n270\t        ):\n271\t            for field_key, field_values in compiled_source.h_field_data.items():\n272\t                if field_key in (\"Hx\", \"Hy\", \"Hz\"):\n273\t                    axis = \"xyz\".index(field_key[1].lower())\n274\t                    # Skip the injection axis component (normal component is not used in equivalence)\n275\t                    if axis == injection_axis:\n276\t                        continue\n277\t                    if len(field_values) == len(compiled_source.placements):\n278\t                        idx = compiled_source.placements.index(placement)\n279\t                        # Magnetic fields contribute to electric field update via J = n × H\n280\t                        # This injects into E along the injection axis\n281\t                        updated_electric[placement][injection_axis] += (\n282\t                            amplitude * weight * direction_sign * field_values[idx]\n283\t                        )\n284\t\n285\t    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n286\t\n287\t\n288\tdef inject_mode_source(\n289\t    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n290\t    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n291\t    compiled_source: CompiledModeSource,\n292\t    *,\n293\t    time: float,\n294\t    dt: float = 1.0,\n295\t) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n296\t    \"\"\"Apply a compiled mode source to the supplied field buffers.\n297\t\n298\t    ModeSource uses the equivalence principle: tangential E and H field\n299\t    components from the mode profile are converted to J and M currents.\n300\t    For direction=\"+\", H fields contribute to E injection and E fields\n301\t    contribute to H injection (forward propagation). For direction=\"-\",\n302\t    the reverse holds (backward propagation).\n303\t\n304\t    Args:\n305\t        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n306\t        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n307\t        compiled_source: The compiled mode source with field data\n308\t        time: Current simulation time\n309\t        dt: Timestep size\n310\t\n311\t    Returns:\n312\t        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n313\t    \"\"\"\n314\t    if dt <= 0.0:\n315\t        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n316\t\n317\t    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n318\t    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n319\t    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n320\t        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n321\t    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n322\t        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n323\t\n324\t    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n325\t    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n326\t    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n327\t\n328\t    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n329\t    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n330\t    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n331\t\n332\t    e_data = compiled_source.e_field_data\n333\t    h_data = compiled_source.h_field_data\n334\t    x_coords = compiled_source.x_coords\n335\t    y_coords = compiled_source.y_coords\n336\t\n337\t    # Get the tangential axes\n338\t    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n339\t    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n340\t\n341\t    # Normalize mode fields for injection (power normalization)\n342\t    power_norm = 1.0 / math.sqrt(compiled_source.mode_power) if compiled_source.mode_power > 0 else 1.0\n343\t\n344\t    for placement, weight in zip(\n345\t        compiled_source.placements,\n346\t        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n347\t        strict=True,\n348\t    ):\n349\t        # Get the local grid position\n350\t        idx_x, idx_y = placement[tang_axis_a], placement[tang_axis_b]\n351\t\n352\t        # Get mode field values at this position (interpolated from mode solver grid)\n353\t        # The mode fields are defined on the cross-sectional grid and are complex\n354\t        ex_val = e_data[\"Ex\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ex\" in e_data else 0.0\n355\t        ey_val = e_data[\"Ey\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ey\" in e_data else 0.0\n356\t        hx_val = h_data[\"Hx\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hx\" in h_data else 0.0\n357\t        hy_val = h_data[\"Hy\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hy\" in h_data else 0.0\n358\t\n359\t        # Apply equivalence principle for mode injection\n360\t        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E, tangential E contributes to H\n361\t        # M = -n × E (electric fields contribute to magnetic current)\n362\t        # J = n × H (magnetic fields contribute to electric current)\n363\t\n364\t        # For a planar source in xy-plane (z-injection):\n365\t        # Jx = n_y * Hz - n_z * Hy = -Hy (for nz=1, ny=0)\n366\t        # Jy = n_z * Hx - n_x * Hz = Hx (for nz=1, nx=0)\n367\t        # Mx = -n_y * Ez + n_z * Ey = Ey (for nz=1, ny=0)\n368\t        # My = -n_z * Ex + n_x * Ez = -Ex (for nz=1, nx=0)\n369\t\n370\t        if direction_sign > 0:\n371\t            # Forward propagation: H fields inject into E, E fields inject into H\n372\t            # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n373\t            # My = -Ex, Mx = Ey for z-injection\n374\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hy_val)\n375\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hx_val\n376\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ey_val\n377\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ex_val)\n378\t        else:\n379\t            # Backward propagation: E fields inject into H, H fields inject into E\n380\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hy_val\n381\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hx_val)\n382\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ey_val)\n383\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ex_val\n384\t\n385\t    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n386\t\n387\t\n388\tdef inject_plane_wave(\n389\t    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n390\t    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n391\t    compiled_source: CompiledPlaneWave,\n392\t    *,\n393\t    time: float,\n394\t    dt: float = 1.0,\n395\t    freq: float | None = None,\n396\t) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n397\t    \"\"\"Apply a compiled plane wave source to the supplied field buffers.\n398\t\n399\t    A PlaneWave injects a spatially uniform electromagnetic wave with a defined\n400\t    propagation direction and polarization. The source uses the equivalence\n401\t    principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E, where n is the source normal.\n402\t\n403\t    For a plane wave, the fields are uniform across the planar injection surface\n404\t    and determined by the k-vector and polarization.\n405\t\n406\t    Args:\n407\t        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n408\t        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n409\t        compiled_source: The compiled plane wave source\n410\t        time: Current simulation time\n411\t        dt: Timestep size\n412\t        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n413\t\n414\t    Returns:\n415\t        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n416\t    \"\"\"\n417\t    if dt <= 0.0:\n418\t        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n419\t\n420\t    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n421\t    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n422\t    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n423\t        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n424\t    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n425\t        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n426\t\n427\t    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n428\t    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n429\t\n430\t    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n431\t    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n432\t    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n433\t\n434\t    # Get tangential axes\n435\t    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n436\t    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n437\t\n438\t    # For a plane wave, the tangential field components are uniform across\n439\t    # the source plane. We compute the E and H fields based on the\n440\t    # polarization and direction vectors.\n441\t\n442\t    # The plane wave fields in the source coordinate system are:\n443\t    # E = E0 * pol_vector * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n444\t    # H = H0 * (k_hat × pol_vector) * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n445\t    #\n446\t    # For injection via equivalence principle:\n447\t    # - The tangential H fields contribute to E via J = n × H\n448\t    # - The tangential E fields contribute to H via M = -n × E\n449\t    #\n450\t    # Since the plane wave is spatially uniform on the injection plane,\n451\t    # we evaluate at a reference point (center of the source plane).\n452\t\n453\t    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n454\t    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n455\t\n456\t    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n457\t    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n458\t    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n459\t\n460\t    # Normalize direction\n461\t    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n462\t    if dir_mag > 0:\n463\t        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n464\t    else:\n465\t        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n466\t\n467\t    # Polarization direction\n468\t    pol_hat = pol_vec\n469\t\n470\t    # For plane wave injection on a planar surface, we inject at all\n471\t    # placements with uniform weight. The plane wave fields are computed\n472\t    # based on the k-vector direction and polarization.\n473\t\n474\t    # The tangential components of E and H that contribute to injection\n475\t    # are determined by the cross products with the injection normal.\n476\t\n477\t    # For n = (0, 0, 1) (z-injection):\n478\t    # J = n × H = (Hx, Hy, 0) → contributes to (Ez,)\n479\t    # M = -n × E = (-Ex, -Ey, 0) → contributes to (Hz,)\n480\t    #\n481\t    # More generally, we need to compute the tangential components of\n482\t    # E and H that are perpendicular to the injection direction.\n483\t\n484\t    # The E field at the source plane is along the polarization direction\n485\t    # The H field is along k_hat × pol_hat (perpendicular to both)\n486\t\n487\t    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n488\t    # H_dir = k_hat × pol_hat\n489\t    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n490\t    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n491\t    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n492\t    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n493\t\n494\t    # The magnitude ratio between E and H in a plane wave is the impedance\n495\t    # For vacuum: Z0 = sqrt(μ0/ε0) ≈ 377 ohms\n496\t    # In terms of normalized units used in FDTD: E/H = Z0\n497\t    z0_normalized = 1.0  # In our normalized units where c=1\n498\t\n499\t    # For a uniform plane wave, the field amplitude is uniform across the plane.\n500\t    # The injection injects the equivalent currents at each cell.\n501\t\n502\t    # For each placement, inject the plane wave fields via equivalence principle\n503\t    for placement, weight in zip(\n504\t        compiled_source.placements,\n505\t        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n506\t        strict=True,\n507\t    ):\n508\t        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n509\t        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight\n510\t        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight\n511\t\n512\t        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n513\t        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n514\t        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n515\t\n516\t        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n517\t        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n518\t        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n519\t\n520\t        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E\n521\t        # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n522\t        # For general injection axis:\n523\t        # The current density J = n × H gives components along tang_axes\n524\t\n525\t        if direction_sign > 0:\n526\t            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n527\t            # E is updated along injection axis from H tangential\n528\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n529\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n530\t            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n531\t            # H is updated along injection axis from E tangential\n532\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n533\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n534\t        else:\n535\t            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n536\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n537\t            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n538\t            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n539\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n540\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n541\t\n542\t    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n543\t\n544\t\n545\tdef inject_gaussian_beam(\n546\t    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n547\t    magnetic_field: np.ndarray]:\n554\t    \"\"\"Apply a compiled Gaussian beam source to the supplied field buffers.\n555\t\n556\t    A GaussianBeam injects a spatially Gaussian-shaped electromagnetic wave with\n557\t    a defined propagation direction, polarization, and waist parameters.\n558\t    The source uses the equivalence principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E.\n559\t\n560\t    The Gaussian envelope determines the amplitude profile across the beam\n561\t    cross-section, with peak amplitude at the center and falling off radially.\n562\t\n563\t    Args:\n564\t        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n565\t        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n566\t        compiled_source: The compiled Gaussian beam source\n567\t        time: Current magnetic_field) buffers\n573\t    \"\"\"\n574\t    if dt <= 0.0:\n575\t        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n576\t\n577\t    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n578\t    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n579\t    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n580\t        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n581\t    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n582\t        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n583\t\n584\t    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n585\t    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n586\t\n587\t    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n588\t    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n589\t    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n590\t\n591\t    # Get tangential axes\n592\t    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n593\t    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n594\t\n595\t    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n596\t    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n597\t\n598\t    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n599\t    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n600\t    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n601\t\n602\t    # Normalize direction\n603\t    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n604\t    if dir_mag > 0:\n605\t        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n606\t    else:\n607\t        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n608\t\n609\t    # Polarization direction\n610\t    pol_hat = pol_vec\n611\t\n612\t    # z0_normalized = 1.0\n620\t\n621\t    # For a Gaussian beam, the field amplitude varies across the beam cross-section\n622\t    # according to the Gaussian envelope. The beam_weights already include this\n623\t    # spatial variation.\n624\t\n625\t    # For each placement, inject the Gaussian beam fields via equivalence principle\n626\t    for placement, placement_weight, beam_weight in zip(\n627\t        compiled_source.placements,\n628\t        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n629\t        compiled_source.beam_weights,\n630\t        strict=True,\n631\t    ):\n632\t        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n640\t        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n641\t        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n642\t\n643\t        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n644\t        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n645\t        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n646\t\n647\t        if direction_sign > 0:\n648\t            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n659\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n660\t            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n661\t\n662\t    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n663\t\n664\t\n665\tdef inject_astigmatic_gaussian_beam(\n666\t    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n667\t    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n668\t    compiled_source: CompiledAstigmaticGaussianBeam,\n669\t    *,\n670\t    time: float,\n671\t    dt: float = 1.0,\n672\t    freq: float | None = None,\n673\t) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n674\t    \"\"\"Apply a compiled astigmatic Gaussian beam source to the supplied field buffers.\n675\t\n676\t    An AstigmaticGaussianBeam injects a spatially Gaussian-shaped electromagnetic wave\n677\t    with separate waist radii in x and y directions. H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n756\t        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n757\t        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n758\t\n759\t        # The incident field is injected at the\n793\t    injection plane, and the TFSF boundary interaction subtracts the incident\n794\t    field at the box edges to maintain the total-field/scattered-field separation.\n795\t\n796\t    The TFSF source works as follows:\n797\t    1. 1.0,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled mode source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    ModeSource uses the equivalence principle: tangential E and H field\n    components from the mode profile are converted to J and M currents.\n    For direction=\"+\", H fields contribute to E injection and E fields\n    contribute to H injection (forward propagation). For direction=\"-\",\n    the reverse holds (backward propagation).\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled mode source with field data\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    e_data = compiled_source.e_field_data\n    h_data = compiled_source.h_field_data\n    x_coords = compiled_source.x_coords\n    y_coords = compiled_source.y_coords\n\n    # Get the tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Normalize mode fields for injection (power normalization)\n    power_norm = 1.0 / math.sqrt(compiled_source.mode_power) if compiled_source.mode_power > 0 else 1.0\n\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # Get the local grid position\n        idx_x, idx_y = placement[tang_axis_a], placement[tang_axis_b]\n\n        # Get mode field values at this position (interpolated from mode solver grid)\n        # The mode fields are defined on the cross-sectional grid and are complex\n        ex_val = e_data[\"Ex\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ex\" in e_data else 0.0\n        ey_val = e_data[\"Ey\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ey\" in e_data else 0.0\n        hx_val = h_data[\"Hx\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hx\" in h_data else 0.0\n        hy_val = h_data[\"Hy\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hy\" in h_data else 0.0\n\n        # Apply equivalence principle for mode injection\n        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E, tangential E contributes to H\n        # M = -n × E (electric fields contribute to magnetic current)\n        # J = n × H (magnetic fields contribute to electric current)\n\n        # For a planar source in xy-plane (z-injection):\n        # Jx = n_y * Hz - n_z * Hy = -Hy (for nz=1, ny=0)\n        # Jy = n_z * Hx - n_x * Hz = Hx (for nz=1, nx=0)\n        # Mx = -n_y * Ez + n_z * Ey = Ey (for nz=1, ny=0)\n        # My = -n_z * Ex + n_x * Ez = -Ex (for nz=1, nx=0)\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward propagation: H fields inject into E, E fields inject into H\n            # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n            # My = -Ex, Mx = Ey for z-injection\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hy_val)\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hx_val\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ey_val\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ex_val)\n        else:\n            # Backward propagation: E fields inject into H, H fields inject into E\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hy_val\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hx_val)\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ey_val)\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ex_val\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_plane_wave(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledPlaneWave,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n    freq: float | None = None,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled plane wave source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    A PlaneWave injects a spatially uniform electromagnetic wave with a defined\n    propagation direction and polarization. The source uses the equivalence\n    principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E, where n is the source normal.\n\n    For a plane wave, the fields are uniform across the planar injection surface\n    and determined by the k-vector and polarization.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled plane wave source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # For a plane wave, the tangential field components are uniform across\n    # the source plane. We compute the E and H fields based on the\n    # polarization and direction vectors.\n\n    # The plane wave fields in the source coordinate system are:\n    # E = E0 * pol_vector * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n    # H = H0 * (k_hat × pol_vector) * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n    #\n    # For injection via equivalence principle:\n    # - The tangential H fields contribute to E via J = n × H\n    # - The tangential E fields contribute to H via M = -n × E\n    #\n    # Since the plane wave is spatially uniform on the injection plane,\n    # we evaluate at a reference point (center of the source plane).\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # For plane wave injection on a planar surface, we inject at all\n    # placements with uniform weight. The plane wave fields are computed\n    # based on the k-vector direction and polarization.\n\n    # The tangential components of E and H that contribute to injection\n    # are determined by the cross products with the injection normal.\n\n    # For n = (0, 0, 1) (z-injection):\n    # J = n × H = (Hx, Hy, 0) → contributes to (Ez,)\n    # M = -n × E = (-Ex, -Ey, 0) → contributes to (Hz,)\n    #\n    # More generally, we need to compute the tangential components of\n    # E and H that are perpendicular to the injection direction.\n\n    # The E field at the source plane is along the polarization direction\n    # The H field is along k_hat × pol_hat (perpendicular to both)\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    # H_dir = k_hat × pol_hat\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # The magnitude ratio between E and H in a plane wave is the impedance\n    # For vacuum: Z0 = sqrt(μ0/ε0) ≈ 377 ohms\n    # In terms of normalized units used in FDTD: E/H = Z0\n    z0_normalized = 1.0  # In our normalized units where c=1\n\n    # For a uniform plane wave, the field amplitude is uniform across the plane.\n    # The injection injects the equivalent currents at each cell.\n\n    # For each placement, inject the plane wave fields via equivalence principle\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n\n        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E\n        # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n        # For general injection axis:\n        # The current density J = n × H gives components along tang_axes\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            # E is updated along injection axis from H tangential\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            # H is updated along injection axis from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_gaussian_beam(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledGaussianBeam,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n    freq: float | None = None,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled Gaussian beam source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    A GaussianBeam injects a spatially Gaussian-shaped electromagnetic wave with\n    a defined propagation direction, polarization, and waist parameters.\n    The source uses the equivalence principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E.\n\n    The Gaussian envelope determines the amplitude profile across the beam\n    cross-section, with peak amplitude at the center and falling off radially.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled Gaussian beam source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # For vacuum impedance in normalized units\n    z0_normalized = 1.0\n\n    # For a Gaussian beam, the field amplitude varies across the beam cross-section\n    # according to the Gaussian envelope. The beam_weights already include this\n    # spatial variation.\n\n    # For each placement, inject the Gaussian beam fields via equivalence principle\n    for placement, placement_weight, beam_weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        compiled_source.beam_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # Combined weight: placement_weight (grid interpolation) * beam_weight (Gaussian envelope)\n        combined_weight = placement_weight * beam_weight\n\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_astigmatic_gaussian_beam(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledAstigmaticGaussianBeam,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n    freq: float | None = None,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled astigmatic Gaussian beam source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    An AstigmaticGaussianBeam injects a spatially Gaussian-shaped electromagnetic wave\n    with separate waist radii in x and y directions. For vacuum impedance in normalized units\n    z0_normalized = 1.0\n\n    # For an astigmatic Gaussian beam, the field amplitude varies with\n    # separate x and y envelope parameters.\n\n    # For each placement, inject the beam fields via equivalence principle\n    for placement, placement_weight, beam_weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        compiled_source.beam_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # The incident field is injected at the\n    injection plane, and the TFSF boundary interaction subtracts the incident\n    field at the box edges to maintain the total-field/scattered-field separation.\n\n    The TFSF source works as follows:\n    1. The incident plane wave is injected at the injection plane using the\n       equivalence principle (same as a plane wave source)\n    2. At the six faces of the TFSF box, the incident field is subtracted\n       from the total field to produce the scattered field outside the box\n\n    For normal incidence, the injection is equivalent to a plane wave. For\n    oblique incidence, the field is projected onto the injection axis.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled TFSF source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # For vacuum impedance in normalized units\n    z0_normalized = 1.0\n\n    # TFSF injection has two components:\n    # 1. Inject the incident field at the injection plane (forward face)\n    # 2. Apply the TF/SF boundary correction at all six faces\n\n    # For Phase 1, we implement the injection component only.\n    # The full TFSF boundary correction (subtracting incident field at the\n    # box edges) requires additional tracking of the incident field and\n    # is deferred to a later task that implements the full TFSF boundary stage.\n\n    # The injection plane is at one face of the TFSF box. For direction=\"+\",\n    # the injection plane is at the negative face of the box along the\n    # injection axis. For direction=\"-\", it's at the positive face.\n\n    # Get TFSF bounds indices\n    (imin, jmin, kmin), (imax, jmax, kmax) = compiled_source.tfsf_bounds_indices\n\n    # For each placement in the TFSF volume, inject the plane wave fields\n    # via equivalence principle (same as plane wave injection)\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along tangential axes\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along tangential axes\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n","numLines":913,"startLine":1,"totalLines":913}}}

complex(self.source.source_time.amp_time(time)[0])\n752\t\n753\t\n754\tdef _plane_wave_k_from_angles(\n755\t    angle_theta: float,\n756\t    angle_phi: float,\n757\t    direction: Literal[\"+\", \"-\"],\n758\t    injection_axis: int,\n759\t    n_medium: float = 1.0,\n760\t) -> tuple[float, float, float]:\n761\t    \"\"\"Compute k-vector components from angles for a plane wave.\n762\t\n763\t    Args:\n764\t        angle_theta: Polar angle from injection axis\n765\t        angle_phi: Azimuth angle in plane perpendicular to injection axis\n766\t        direction: Propagation direction along injection axis (+ or -)\n767\t        injection_axis: Which axis is the injection normal (0=x, 1=y, 2=z)\n768\t        n_medium: Refractive index of the background medium\n769\t\n770\t    Returns:\n771\t        kx, ky, kz components of the wavevector\n772\t    \"\"\"\n773\t    c0 = 2.998e8  # speed of light in vacuum\n774\t\n775\t    # Direction multiplier\n776\t    dir_mult = 1.0 if direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n777\t\n778\t    # For a plane wave propagating at angles theta, phi relative to injection axis:\n779\t    # kx = k * sin(theta) * cos(phi)\n780\t    # ky = k * sin(theta) * sin(phi)\n781\t    # kz = k * cos(theta) for injection along z\n782\t    # Then we rotate to the actual injection axis\n783\t\n784\t    sin_theta = math.sin(angle_theta)\n785\t    cos_theta = math.cos(angle_theta)\n786\t    sin_phi = math.sin(angle_phi)\n787\t    cos_phi = math.cos(angle_phi)\n788\t\n789\t    # k magnitude in the medium\n790\t    k_mag = dir_mult * n_medium * 2 * math.pi / c0\n791\t\n792\t    # Temporary k vector assuming injection along z\n793\t    kx_t = k_mag * sin_theta * cos_phi\n794\t    ky_t = k_mag * sin_theta * sin_phi\n795\t    kz_t = k_mag * cos_theta\n796\t\n797\t    # Rotate to actual injection axis\n798\t    if injection_axis == 0:\n799\t        # Injection along x: kx=z, ky=y, kz=x -> we need kz=kx_t, kx=ky_t, ky=kz_t\n800\t        return (kz_t, ky_t, kx_t)\n801\t    elif injection_axis == 1:\n802\t        # Injection along y: kx=x, ky=z, kz=y -> we need kx=kx_t, ky=kz_t, kz=ky_t\n803\t        return (kx_t, kz_t, ky_t)\n804\t    else:\n805\t        # Injection along z: kx=x, ky=y, kz=z\n806\t        return (kx_t, ky_t, kz_t)\n807\t\n808\t\n809\tdef compile_plane_wave(\n810\t    source: PlaneWave,\n811\t    *,\n812\t    grid:          ):\n851\t                placements.append((x_index, y_index, z_index))\n852\t                placement_weights.append(float(x_weight * y_weight * z_weight))\n853\t\n854\t    # Angular spec )\n858\t\n859\t    # The k-vector functions will be evaluated at runtime per frequency\n860\t    # For now, store the parameters for later evaluation\n861\t    def _make_k_funcs(inj_axis: int, direction: Literal[\"+\", \"-\"]):\n862\t        \"\"\"Create k-vector functions for the plane wave at a given frequency.\"\"\"\n863\t\n864\t        def kx_func(freq: float, n: float = 1.0) -> float:\n865\t            kx, _, _ = _plane_wave_k_from_angles(\n866\t                source.angle_theta, source.angle_phi, direction, inj_axis, n\n867\t            )\n868\t            return kx / (2 * math.pi * freq) if freq > 0 else 0.0\n869\t\n870\t        def ky_func(freq: float, n: float = 1.0) -> float:\n871\t            _, ky, _ = _plane_wave_k_from_angles(\n872\t                source.angle_theta, source.angle_phi, direction, inj_axis, n\n873\t            )\n874\t            return ky / (2 * math.pi * freq) if freq > 0 else 0.0\n875\t\n876\t        def kz_func(freq: float, CompiledAxisPlacement],\n954\t    injection_axis: int,\n955\t    waist_radius: float,\n956\t    waist_distance: float,\n957\t) -> tuple[float, ...]:\n958\t    \"\"\"Compute Gaussian envelope weights for beam cross-section.\n959\t\n960\t    Args:\n961\t        axis_placements: The compiled axis placements for x, y, z\n962\t        injection_axis: The injection axis (0=x, 1=y, 2=z)\n963\t \"\"\"\n969\t    # Get the two tangential axes\n970\t    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n971\t\n972\t    # Extract centers for tangential axes\n973\t    centers = [\n974\t        axis_placements[tang_axes[0]].centers,\n975\t        axis_placements[tang_axes[1]].centers,\n976\t    ]\n977\t\n978\t    # Rayleigh range from waist radius: z_R = pi * w0^2 / lambda\n979\t    # For now, we use a simplified model where the Gaussian profile\n980\t    # is computed in the transverse plane at the injection location\n981\t    weights: list[float] = w^2)\n990\t            weight = math.exp(-2.0 * r_squared / (waist_radius * waist_radius))\n991\t            weights.append(weight)\n992\t\n993\t    return tuple(weights)\n994\t\n995\t\n996\tdef return (lower, min(values.size - 1, upper))\n\n\ndef _compile_axis_placement(\n    axis: ResolvedGridAxis,\n    *,\n    center: float,\n    size: float,\n    interpolate: bool,\n    confine_to_bounds: bool,\n) -> CompiledAxisPlacement:\n    if axis.num_cells <= 0:\n        raise ValueError(\"UniformCurrentSource requires a non-collapsed grid axis\")\n\n    centers = _axis_centers(axis)\n    cell_sizes = _axis_sizes(axis)\n    boundaries = np.asarray(axis.boundaries, dtype=float)\n    tolerance = max(1e-12, axis.max_step * 1e-9)\n\n    if math.isclose(size, 0.0, abs_tol=tolerance):\n        exact = np.nonzero(np.isclose(centers, center, rtol=0.0, atol=tolerance))[0]\n        if exact.size:\n            index = int(exact[0])\n            return CompiledAxisPlacement(\n                indices=(index,),\n                weights=(1.0,),\n                cell_sizes=(float(cell_sizes[index]),),\n                centers=(float(centers[index]),),\n            )\n        if not interpolate:\n            index = int(np.argmin(np.abs(centers - center)))\n            return CompiledAxisPlacement(\n                indices=(index,),\n                weights=(1.0,),\n                cell_sizes=(float(cell_sizes[index]),),\n                centers=(float(centers[index]),),\n            )\n\n        lower, upper = _bracket_indices(centers, center)\n        if lower == upper:\n            return CompiledAxisPlacement(\n                indices=(lower,),\n                weights=(1.0,),\n                cell_sizes=(float(cell_sizes[lower]),),\n                centers=(float(centers[lower]),),\n            )\n        left_center = centers[lower]\n        right_center = centers[upper]\n        fraction = float((center - left_center) / (right_center - left_center))\n        return CompiledAxisPlacement(\n            indices=(lower, upper),\n            weights=(1.0 - fraction, fraction),\n            cell_sizes=(float(cell_sizes[lower]), float(cell_sizes[upper])),\n            centers=(float(left_center), float(right_center)),\n        )\n\n    lower = center - size / 2.0\n    upper = center + size / 2.0\n    if confine_to_bounds:\n        overlaps = np.minimum(boundaries[1:], upper) - np.maximum(boundaries[:-1], lower)\n        mask = overlaps > tolerance\n        if not np.any(mask):\n            raise ValueError(\"UniformCurrentSource support does not overlap the resolved grid\")\n        weights = overlaps[mask] / cell_sizes[mask]\n        indices = np.nonzero(mask)[0]\n    else:\n        mask = (centers >= lower - tolerance) & (centers <= upper + tolerance)\n        if not np.any(mask):\n            raise ValueError(\"UniformCurrentSource support does not include any resolved grid cells\")\n        weights = np.ones(int(np.count_nonzero(mask)), dtype=float)\n        indices = np.nonzero(mask)[0]\n    return CompiledAxisPlacement(\n        indices=tuple(int(index) for index in indices),\n        weights=tuple(float(weight) for weight in weights),\n        cell_sizes=tuple(float(cell_sizes[index]) for index in indices),\n        centers=tuple(float(centers[index]) for index in indices),\n    )\n\n\ndef _transverse_extent(source: UniformCurrentSource, grid: ResolvedGrid) -> float:\n    placements = []\n    axes = (grid.x, grid.y, grid.z)\n    for axis in range(3):\n measure = 1.0\n    for extent in placements:\n        measure *= extent\n    return measure\n\n\ndef compile_uniform_current_source(\n    source: UniformCurrentSource,\n    *,\n    grid: tuple[float, float, float]]\n    injection_axis: int\n    direction: Literal[\"+\", \"-\"]\n    angle_theta: float\n    angle_phi: float\n    pol_angle: float\n    dir_vector: tuple[float, float, frequency\n    ky_func: object  # Callable that returns ky at a given frequency\n    kz_func: object  # Callable that returns kz at a given frequency\n    # Angular spec type\n    angular_spec_type: Literal[\"FixedInPlaneK\", \"FixedAngle\"]\n\n    @property\n    def placement_kind(self) -> Literal[\"sheet\"]:\n        \"\"\"Always a sheet (planar) placement.\"\"\"\n        return \"sheet\"\n\n    @property\n    def support_point_count(self) -> int:\n        return len(self.placements)\n\n    def amplitude_at_time(self, time: float) -> complex:\n        return complex(self.source.source_time.amp_time(time)[0])\n\n\ndef _plane_wave_k_from_angles(\n    angle_theta: float,\n    angle_phi: float,\n    direction: Literal[\"+\", \"-\"],\n    injection_axis: int,\n    n_medium: float = 1.0,\n) -> tuple[float, float, float]:\n    \"\"\"Compute k-vector components from angles for a plane wave.\n\n    Args:\n        angle_theta: Polar angle from injection axis\n        angle_phi: Azimuth angle in plane perpendicular to injection axis\n        direction: Propagation direction along injection axis (+ or -)\n        injection_axis: Which axis is the injection normal (0=x, 1=y, 2=z)\n        n_medium: Refractive index of the background medium\n\n    Returns:\n        kx, ky, kz components of the wavevector\n    \"\"\"\n    c0 = 2.998e8  # speed of light in vacuum\n\n    # Direction multiplier\n    dir_mult = 1.0 if direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n\n    # For a plane wave propagating at angles theta, phi relative to injection axis:\n    # kx = k * sin(theta) * cos(phi)\n    # ky = k * sin(theta) * sin(phi)\n    # kz = k * cos(theta) for injection along z\n    # Then we rotate to the actual injection axis\n\n    sin_theta = math.sin(angle_theta)\n    cos_theta = math.cos(angle_theta)\n    sin_phi = math.sin(angle_phi)\n    cos_phi = math.cos(angle_phi)\n\n    # k magnitude in the medium\n    k_mag = dir_mult * n_medium * 2 * math.pi / c0\n\n    # Temporary k vector assuming injection along z\n    kx_t = k_mag * sin_theta * cos_phi\n    ky_t = k_mag * sin_theta * sin_phi\n    kz_t = k_mag * cos_theta\n\n    # Rotate to actual injection axis\n    if injection_axis == 0:\n        # Injection along x: kx=z, ky=y, kz=x -> we need kz=kx_t, kx=ky_t, ky=kz_t\n        return (kz_t, ky_t, kx_t)\n    elif injection_axis == 1:\n        # Injection along y: kx=x, ky=z, kz=y -> we need kx=kx_t, ky=kz_t, kz=ky_t\n        return (kx_t, kz_t, ky_t)\n    else:\n        # Injection along z: kx=x, ky=y, kz=z\n        return (kx_t, ky_t, kz_t)\n\n\ndef compile_plane_wave(\n    source: PlaneWave,\n    *,\n    grid: ResolvedGrid,\n) -> CompiledPlaneWave:\n    \"\"\"Compile a plane wave source onto the resolved primal-cell grid.\n\n    Args:\n        source: The PlaneWave to compile\n        grid: The resolved simulation grid\n\n    Returns:\n        CompiledPlaneWave with placement and direction data\n    \"\"\"\n    axis_placements = tuple(\n        _compile_axis_placement(\n            axis_grid,\n            center=source.center[axis],\n            size=source.size[axis],\n            interpolate=source.interpolate,\n            confine_to_bounds=source.confine_to_bounds,\n        )\n        for axis, axis_grid in enumerate((grid.x, grid.y, grid.z))\n    )\n\n    placements: list[tuple[int, int, int]] = []\n    placement_weights: list[float] = []\n    for x_index, x_weight in zip(\n        axis_placements[0].indices,\n        axis_placements[0].weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        for y_index, y_weight in zip(\n            axis_placements[1].indices,\n            axis_placements[1].weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for z_index, z_weight in zip(\n                axis_placements[2].indices,\n                axis_placements[2].weights,\n                strict=True,\n            ):\n                placements.append((x_index, y_index, z_index))\n                placement_weights.append(float(x_weight * y_weight * z_weight))\n\n    # Angular spec type for frequency handling\n    angular_spec_type: Literal[\"FixedInPlaneK\", \"FixedAngle\"] = (\n        \"FixedAngle\" if source.is_fixed_angle else \"FixedInPlaneK\"\n    )\n\n    # The k-vector functions will be evaluated at runtime per frequency\n    # For now, store the parameters for later evaluation\n    def _make_k_funcs(inj_axis: int, direction: Literal[\"+\", \"-\"]):\n        \"\"\"Create k-vector functions for the plane wave at a given frequency.\"\"\"\n\n        def kx_func(freq: float, n: float = 1.0) -> float:\n            kx, _, _ = _plane_wave_k_from_angles(\n                source.angle_theta, Hz[i,j,k])/dy - (Hy[i,j,k+1] - Hy[i,j,k])/dz\n26\t    )\n27\t    Ey[i+1] = Ey[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n28\t        (Hx[i,j,k+1] - Hx[i,j,k])/dz - (Hz[i+1,j,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dx\n29\t    )\n30\t    Ez[i+1] = Ez[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n31\t        (Hy[i+1,j,k] - Ey[i,j,k])/dz\n38\t    )\n39\t    Hy[i+1] = Hy[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n40\t        (Ex[i,j,k+1] - Ex[i,j,k])/dz - (Ez[i+1,j,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dx\n41\t    )\n42\t    Hz[i+1] = Hz[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n43\t        (Ey[i+1,j,k] - Ey[i,j,k])/dx - (Ex[i,j+1,k] - Ex[i,j,k])/dy\n44\t    )\n45\t\n46\tCoefficients\n47\t-----------\n48\tFor efficiency, the coefficients are precomputed as::\n49\t\n50\t    ce_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for E update\n51\t    ce_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for H update (note the sign)\n52\t    cm_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for magnetic current (same as ce_e)\n53\t    cm_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for magnetic update (same as ce_h)\n54\t\n55\tIn a homogeneous medium these are constants. In an inhomogeneous medium\n56\tthey vary spatially and are sampled from coefficient arrays.\n57\t\n58\tCapture-Safe Stepping\n59\t---------------------\n60\tAll timestep-dependent values are passed as explicit kernel arguments:\n61\t\n62\t- ``dt``: scalar float timestep\n63\t- ``step_index``: int step counter (for time-varying sources)\n64\t- ``time``: float current simulation time\n65\t\n66\tSource amplitudes are computed in Python and passed as scalar or array\n67\targuments. Hz[i,j,k])/dy - (Hy[i,j,k+1] - Hy[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Ey[i+1] = Ey[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hx[i,j,k+1] - Hx[i,j,k])/dz - (Hz[i+1,j,k] - Hz[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Ez[i+1] = Ez[i] + dt/(eps[i]*eps0) * (\n        (Hy[i+1,j,k] - Hy[i,j,k])/dx - (Hx[i,j+1,k] - Hx[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nMagnetic field update::\n\n    Hx[i+1] = Hx[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ez[i,j+1,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dy - (Ey[i,j,k+1] - Ey[i,j,k])/dz\n    )\n    Hy[i+1] = Hy[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ex[i,j,k+1] - Ex[i,j,k])/dz - (Ez[i+1,j,k] - Ez[i,j,k])/dx\n    )\n    Hz[i+1] = Hz[i] - dt/(mu[i]*mu0) * (\n        (Ey[i+1,j,k] - Ey[i,j,k])/dx - (Ex[i,j+1,k] - Ex[i,j,k])/dy\n    )\n\nCoefficients\n-----------\nFor efficiency, the coefficients are precomputed as::\n\n    ce_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for E update\n    ce_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for H update (note the sign)\n    cm_e = dt / (eps * eps0)    # for magnetic current (same as ce_e)\n    cm_h = -dt / (mu * mu0)     # for magnetic update (same as ce_h)\n\nIn a homogeneous medium these are constants. In an inhomogeneous medium\nthey vary spatially and are sampled from coefficient arrays.\n\nCapture-Safe Stepping\n---------------------\nAll timestep-dependent values are passed as explicit kernel arguments:\n\n- ``dt``: scalar float timestep\n- ``step_index``: int step counter (for time-varying sources)\n- ``time``: float current simulation time\n\nSource amplitudes are computed in Python and passed as scalar or array\narguments. No closure captures are used in kernel definitions.\n\nReferences\n---------\n- Vacuum update: ``../meep/src/update_eh.cpp``\n- Kernel formula sources: May be near zero at t=0\n\n\ndef test_tfsf_reference_wavelength():\n    \"\"\"TFSF correctly computes reference wavelength from source_time.\"\"\"\n    # With GaussianPulse\n    tfsf_g = TFSF(\n        center=(0.0, 0.0, 0.0),\n        size=(2.0, 2.0, 2.0),\n        source_time=GaussianPulse(freq0=2.0, fwidth=0.5),\n        injection_axis=2,\n        direction=\"+\",\n    )\n    wavelength_g = tfsf_g._reference_wavelength\n    assert wavelength_g is not None\n    assert abs(wavelength_g - 2.998e8 / 2.0) < 1e6  # c/freq\n\n    # With ContinuousWave\n    tfsf_cw = TFSF(\n        center=(0.0, 0.0, 0.0),\n        size=(2.0, 2.0, 2.0),\n        source_time=ContinuousWave(freq0=1.5, fwidth=0.5, amplitude=1.0),\n        injection_axis=2,\n        direction=\"+\",\n    )\n    wavelength_cw = tfsf_cw._reference_wavelength\n    assert wavelength_cw is not None\n    assert abs(wavelength_cw - 2.998e8 / 1.5) < 1e6\n\n\ndef test_tfsf_tfsf_bounds():\n    \"\"\"TFSF correctly computes tfsf_bounds property.\"\"\"\n    tfsf = TFSF(\n        center=(5.0, 5.0, 5.0),\n        size=(2.0, 4.0, 6.0),\n        source_time=GaussianPulse(freq0=1.0, fwidth=0.5),\n        injection_axis=2,\n        direction=\"+\",\n    )\n\n    lower, upper = tfsf.tfsf_bounds\n    assert lower == (4.0, 3.0, 2.0)\n    assert upper == (6.0, 7.0, 8.0)\n\n\ndef test_tfsf_tangential_axes():\n    \"\"\"TFSF correctly identifies tangential axes.\"\"\"\n    # timestep. It applies all source types in the following order:\n\n    1. Uniform current sources (electric and magnetic currents)\n    2. Point dipole sources\n    3. Custom current sources\n    4. Custom field sources (equivalence principle)\n    5. Mode sources (equivalence principle with mode profile)\n    6. Plane wave sources (equivalence principle)\n    7. Gaussian beam sources (equivalence principle with Gaussian envelope)\n    8. Astigmatic Gaussian beam sources\n    9. TFSF sources (volume injection with TF/SF correction)\n\n    Scheduling Semantics\n    --------------------\n    The source_injection stage runs after the first boundary_exchange and\n    electric_update, and before the second boundary_exchange and magnetic_update::\n\n        boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection\n        → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → pml_stage\n\n    All sources receive the same (time, dt, freq) arguments so that sources\n    of different types remain synchronized. The caller is responsible for\n    passing consistent time values across the stage sequence.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    electric_field : np.ndarray\n        E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n    magnetic_field : np.ndarray\n        H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3).\n    uniform_current_sources : tuple[CompiledUniformCurrentSource, ...]\n        Compiled uniform current sources.\n    point_dipole_sources : tuple[CompiledPointDipole, ...]\n        Compiled point dipole sources.\n    custom_current_sources : tuple[CompiledCustomCurrentSource, ...]\n        Compiled custom current sources.\n    custom_field_sources : tuple[CompiledCustomFieldSource, ...]\n        Compiled custom field sources.\n    mode_sources : tuple[CompiledModeSource, ...]\n        Compiled mode sources.\n    plane_wave_sources : tuple[CompiledPlaneWave, ...]\n        Compiled plane wave sources.\n    gaussian_beam_sources : tuple[CompiledGaussianBeam, ...]\n        Compiled Gaussian beam sources.\n    astigmatic_gaussian_beam_sources : tuple[CompiledAstigmaticGaussianBeam, ...]\n        Compiled astigmatic Gaussian beam sources.\n    tfsf_sources : tuple[CompiledTFSF, ...]\n        Compiled TFSF sources.\n    time : float\n        Current simulation time.\n    dt : float\n        Timestep size.\n    freq : float, optional\n        Frequency for frequency-dependent sources (e.g., plane waves with\n        FixedInPlaneKSpec).\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers.\n    \"\"\"\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n\n    # Uniform current sources (electric/magnetic currents)\n    if uniform_current_sources:\n        electric, magnetic = apply_uniform_current_sources(\n            electric, magnetic, uniform_current_sources, time=time, dt=dt\n        )\n\n    # Point dipole sources\n    if point_dipole_sources:\n        electric, magnetic = apply_point_dipole_sources(\n            electric, magnetic, point_dipole_sources, time=time, dt=dt\n        )\n\n    # Custom current sources\n    if custom_current_sources:\n        electric, magnetic = apply_custom_current_sources(\n            electric, magnetic, custom_current_sources, time=time, dt=dt\n        )\n\n    # Custom field sources (equivalence principle)\n    if custom_field_sources:\n        electric, magnetic = apply_custom_field_source_sources(\n            electric, magnetic, custom_field_sources, time=time, dt=dt\n        )\n\n    # Mode sources (equivalence principle with mode profile)\n    if mode_sources:\n        electric, magnetic = apply_mode_sources(\n            electric, magnetic, mode_sources, time=time, dt=dt\n        )\n\n    # Plane wave sources (equivalence principle)\n    if plane_wave_sources:\n        electric, magnetic = apply_plane_wave_sources(\n            electric, magnetic, plane_wave_sources, time=time, dt=dt, freq=freq\n        )\n\n    # Gaussian beam sources (equivalence principle with Gaussian envelope)\n    if gaussian_beam_sources:\n        electric, magnetic = apply_gaussian_beam_sources(\n            electric, magnetic, gaussian_beam_sources, time=time, dt=dt, freq=freq\n        )\n\n    # Astigmatic Gaussian beam sources\n    if astigmatic_gaussian_beam_sources:\n        electric, magnetic = apply_astigmatic_gaussian_beam_sources(\n            electric, magnetic, astigmatic_gaussian_beam_sources, time=time, dt=dt, freq=freq\n        )\n\n    # TFSF sources (volume injection with TF/SF correction)\n    if tfsf_sources:\n        electric, magnetic = apply_tfsf_sources(\n            electric, magnetic, tfsf_sources, time=time, dt=dt\n        )\n\n    return electric, magnetic"}}],"model":"MiniMax-M2.7","stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":73710,"output_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":0,"cache_read_input_tokens":23360},"service_tier":"standard","context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"3aeecfc1-a319-4311-bc41-2135839dedf2","uuid":"424f3624-9310-4cab-93c8-b56f99a6b165"}

The incident field is injected at the\n    injection plane, and the TFSF boundary interaction subtracts the incident\n    field at the box edges to maintain the total-field/scattered-field separation.\n\n    The TFSF source works as follows:\n    1. Inject the incident field at the injection plane (forward face)\n    # 2. Apply the TF/SF boundary correction at all six faces\n\n    # For Phase 1, we implement the injection component only.\n    # The full TFSF boundary correction (subtracting incident field at the\n    # box edges) requires additional tracking of the incident field and\n    # is deferred to a later task that implements the full TFSF boundary stage.\n\n    # The injection plane is at one face of the TFSF box. For direction=\"+\",\n    # the injection plane is at the negative face of the box along the\n    # injection axis. For direction=\"-\", it's at the positive face.\n\n    # Get TFSF bounds indices\n    (imin, jmin, kmin), (imax, jmax, kmax) = compiled_source.tfsf_bounds_indices\n\n    # For each placement in the TFSF volume, inject the plane wave fields\n    # via equivalence principle (same as plane wave injection)\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along tangential axes\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along tangential axes\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n","numLines":134,"startLine":780,"totalLines":913}}}
