compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        target[placement][axis] += amplitude * weight\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_custom_current_source(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledCustomCurrentSource,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled custom current source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    CustomCurrentSource can inject both electric and magnetic field components\n    based on the provided field data arrays.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Apply electric field components\n    if compiled_source.has_electric and compiled_source.e_field_data is not None:\n        for placement, weight in zip(\n            compiled_source.placements,\n            compiled_source.placement_weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for field_key, field_values in compiled_source.e_field_data.items():\n                if field_key in (\"Ex\", \"Ey\", \"Ez\"):\n                    axis = \"xyz\".index(field_key[1].lower())\n                    # Apply field data - simplified version using index mapping\n                    if len(field_values) == len(compiled_source.placements):\n                        idx = compiled_source.placements.index(placement)\n                        updated_electric[placement][axis] += amplitude * weight * field_values[idx]\n\n    # Apply magnetic field components\n    if compiled_source.has_magnetic and compiled_source.h_field_data is not None:\n        for placement, weight in zip(\n            compiled_source.placements,\n            compiled_source.placement_weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for field_key, field_values in compiled_source.h_field_data.items():\n                if field_key in (\"Hx\", \"Hy\", \"Hz\"):\n                    axis = \"xyz\".index(field_key[1].lower())\n                    if len(field_values) == len(compiled_source.placements):\n                        idx = compiled_source.placements.index(placement)\n                        updated_magnetic[placement][axis] += amplitude * weight * field_values[idx]\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_custom_field_source(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledCustomFieldSource,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled custom field source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    CustomFieldSource uses the equivalence principle on a planar surface.\n    For tangential field components provided:\n    - Electric field components (Ex, Ey on xy-plane) contribute via M = -n × E\n    - Magnetic field components (Hx, Hy on xy-plane) contribute via J = n × H\n\n    The direction sign determines whether the source injects forward (+)\n    or backward (-) relative to the injection axis normal.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward, -1 for backward\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n\n    # Injection axis determines the normal direction for equivalence principle\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Apply electric field components via equivalence principle: M = -n × E\n    # For a planar source, tangential E fields contribute to H update\n    if compiled_source.has_electric and compiled_source.e_field_data is not None:\n        for placement, weight in zip(\n            compiled_source.placements,\n            compiled_source.placement_weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for field_key, field_values in compiled_source.e_field_data.items():\n                if field_key in (\"Ex\", \"Ey\", \"Ez\"):\n                    axis = \"xyz\".index(field_key[1].lower())\n                    # Skip the injection axis component (normal component is not used in equivalence)\n                    if axis == injection_axis:\n                        continue\n                    if len(field_values) == len(compiled_source.placements):\n                        idx = compiled_source.placements.index(placement)\n                        # Electric fields contribute to magnetic field update via M = -n × E\n                        # This injects into H along the injection axis\n                        updated_magnetic[placement][injection_axis] += (\n                            amplitude * weight * direction_sign * field_values[idx]\n                        )\n\n    # Apply magnetic field components via equivalence principle: J = n × H\n    # For a planar source, tangential H fields contribute to E update\n    if compiled_source.has_magnetic and compiled_source.h_field_data is not None:\n        for placement, weight in zip(\n            compiled_source.placements,\n            compiled_source.placement_weights,\n            strict=True,\n        ):\n            for field_key, field_values in compiled_source.h_field_data.items():\n                if field_key in (\"Hx\", \"Hy\", \"Hz\"):\n                    axis = \"xyz\".index(field_key[1].lower())\n                    # Skip the injection axis component (normal component is not used in equivalence)\n                    if axis == injection_axis:\n                        continue\n                    if len(field_values) == len(compiled_source.placements):\n                        idx = compiled_source.placements.index(placement)\n                        # Magnetic fields contribute to electric field update via J = n × H\n                        # This injects into E along the injection axis\n                        updated_electric[placement][injection_axis] += (\n                            amplitude * weight * direction_sign * field_values[idx]\n                        )\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_mode_source(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledModeSource,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled mode source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    ModeSource uses the equivalence principle: tangential E and H field\n    components from the mode profile are converted to J and M currents.\n    For direction=\"+\", H fields contribute to E injection and E fields\n    contribute to H injection (forward propagation). For direction=\"-\",\n    the reverse holds (backward propagation).\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled mode source with field data\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    e_data = compiled_source.e_field_data\n    h_data = compiled_source.h_field_data\n    x_coords = compiled_source.x_coords\n    y_coords = compiled_source.y_coords\n\n    # Get the tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Normalize mode fields for injection (power normalization)\n    power_norm = 1.0 / math.sqrt(compiled_source.mode_power) if compiled_source.mode_power > 0 else 1.0\n\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # Get the local grid position\n        idx_x, idx_y = placement[tang_axis_a], placement[tang_axis_b]\n\n        # Get mode field values at this position (interpolated from mode solver grid)\n        # The mode fields are defined on the cross-sectional grid and are complex\n        ex_val = e_data[\"Ex\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ex\" in e_data else 0.0\n        ey_val = e_data[\"Ey\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Ey\" in e_data else 0.0\n        hx_val = h_data[\"Hx\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hx\" in h_data else 0.0\n        hy_val = h_data[\"Hy\"][idx_x, idx_y] if \"Hy\" in h_data else 0.0\n\n        # Apply equivalence principle for mode injection\n        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E, tangential E contributes to H\n        # M = -n × E (electric fields contribute to magnetic current)\n        # J = n × H (magnetic fields contribute to electric current)\n\n        # For a planar source in xy-plane (z-injection):\n        # Jx = n_y * Hz - n_z * Hy = -Hy (for nz=1, ny=0)\n        # Jy = n_z * Hx - n_x * Hz = Hx (for nz=1, nx=0)\n        # Mx = -n_y * Ez + n_z * Ey = Ey (for nz=1, ny=0)\n        # My = -n_z * Ex + n_x * Ez = -Ex (for nz=1, nx=0)\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward propagation: H fields inject into E, E fields inject into H\n            # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n            # My = -Ex, Mx = Ey for z-injection\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hy_val)\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hx_val\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ey_val\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ex_val)\n        else:\n            # Backward propagation: E fields inject into H, H fields inject into E\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * hy_val\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-hx_val)\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * (-ey_val)\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += amplitude * weight * power_norm * ex_val\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_plane_wave(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledPlaneWave,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n    freq: float | None = None,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled plane wave source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    A PlaneWave injects a spatially uniform electromagnetic wave with a defined\n    propagation direction and polarization. The source uses the equivalence\n    principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E, where n is the source normal.\n\n    For a plane wave, the fields are uniform across the planar injection surface\n    and determined by the k-vector and polarization.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled plane wave source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # For a plane wave, the tangential field components are uniform across\n    # the source plane. We compute the E and H fields based on the\n    # polarization and direction vectors.\n\n    # The plane wave fields in the source coordinate system are:\n    # E = E0 * pol_vector * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n    # H = H0 * (k_hat × pol_vector) * exp(i * (k · r - ωt))\n    #\n    # For injection via equivalence principle:\n    # - The tangential H fields contribute to E via J = n × H\n    # - The tangential E fields contribute to H via M = -n × E\n    #\n    # Since the plane wave is spatially uniform on the injection plane,\n    # we evaluate at a reference point (center of the source plane).\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # For plane wave injection on a planar surface, we inject at all\n    # placements with uniform weight. The plane wave fields are computed\n    # based on the k-vector direction and polarization.\n\n    # The tangential components of E and H that contribute to injection\n    # are determined by the cross products with the injection normal.\n\n    # For n = (0, 0, 1) (z-injection):\n    # J = n × H = (Hx, Hy, 0) → contributes to (Ez,)\n    # M = -n × E = (-Ex, -Ey, 0) → contributes to (Hz,)\n    #\n    # More generally, we need to compute the tangential components of\n    # E and H that are perpendicular to the injection direction.\n\n    # The E field at the source plane is along the polarization direction\n    # The H field is along k_hat × pol_hat (perpendicular to both)\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    # H_dir = k_hat × pol_hat\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # The magnitude ratio between E and H in a plane wave is the impedance\n    # For vacuum: Z0 = sqrt(μ0/ε0) ≈ 377 ohms\n    # In terms of normalized units used in FDTD: E/H = Z0\n    z0_normalized = 1.0  # In our normalized units where c=1\n\n    # For a uniform plane wave, the field amplitude is uniform across the plane.\n    # The injection injects the equivalent currents at each cell.\n\n    # For each placement, inject the plane wave fields via equivalence principle\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n\n        # For + direction: tangential H contributes to E\n        # Jy = Hx, Jx = -Hy for z-injection\n        # For general injection axis:\n        # The current density J = n × H gives components along tang_axes\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            # E is updated along injection axis from H tangential\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            # H is updated along injection axis from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_gaussian_beam(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledGaussianBeam,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n    freq: float | None = None,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled Gaussian beam source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    A GaussianBeam injects a spatially Gaussian-shaped electromagnetic wave with\n    a defined propagation direction, polarization, and waist parameters.\n    The source uses the equivalence principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E.\n\n    The Gaussian envelope determines the amplitude profile across the beam\n    cross-section, with peak amplitude at the center and falling off radially.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled Gaussian beam source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # For vacuum impedance in normalized units\n    z0_normalized = 1.0\n\n    # For a Gaussian beam, the field amplitude varies across the beam cross-section\n    # according to the Gaussian envelope. The beam_weights already include this\n    # spatial variation.\n\n    # For each placement, inject the Gaussian beam fields via equivalence principle\n    for placement, placement_weight, beam_weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        compiled_source.beam_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # Combined weight: placement_weight (grid interpolation) * beam_weight (Gaussian envelope)\n        combined_weight = placement_weight * beam_weight\n\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_astigmatic_gaussian_beam(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledAstigmaticGaussianBeam,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n    freq: float | None = None,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled astigmatic Gaussian beam source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    An AstigmaticGaussianBeam injects a spatially Gaussian-shaped electromagnetic wave\n    with separate waist radii in x and y directions. The source uses the equivalence\n    principle: J = n × H and M = -n × E.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled astigmatic Gaussian beam source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n        freq: Frequency for k-vector evaluation (for FixedInPlaneKSpec)\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # For vacuum impedance in normalized units\n    z0_normalized = 1.0\n\n    # For an astigmatic Gaussian beam, the field amplitude varies with\n    # separate x and y envelope parameters.\n\n    # For each placement, inject the beam fields via equivalence principle\n    for placement, placement_weight, beam_weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        compiled_source.beam_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # Combined weight: placement_weight (grid interpolation) * beam_weight (Gaussian envelope)\n        combined_weight = placement_weight * beam_weight\n\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * combined_weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along injection axis\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along injection axis\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # Backward: E tangential contributes to H via M = -n × E\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n\n    return updated_electric, updated_magnetic\n\n\ndef inject_tfsf(\n    electric_field: np.ndarray,\n    magnetic_field: np.ndarray,\n    compiled_source: CompiledTFSF,\n    *,\n    time: float,\n    dt: float = 1.0,\n) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:\n    \"\"\"Apply a compiled TFSF source to the supplied field buffers.\n\n    A TFSF (Total-Field Scattered-Field) source injects a plane wave in a finite\n    region of the simulation domain. The incident field is injected at the\n    injection plane, and the TFSF boundary interaction subtracts the incident\n    field at the box edges to maintain the total-field/scattered-field separation.\n\n    The TFSF source works as follows:\n    1. The incident plane wave is injected at the injection plane using the\n       equivalence principle (same as a plane wave source)\n    2. At the six faces of the TFSF box, the incident field is subtracted\n       from the total field to produce the scattered field outside the box\n\n    For normal incidence, the injection is equivalent to a plane wave. For\n    oblique incidence, the field is projected onto the injection axis.\n\n    Args:\n        electric_field: The E field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        magnetic_field: The H field buffer with shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\n        compiled_source: The compiled TFSF source\n        time: Current simulation time\n        dt: Timestep size\n\n    Returns:\n        Updated (electric_field, magnetic_field) buffers\n    \"\"\"\n    if dt <= 0.0:\n        raise ValueError(\"dt must be positive\")\n\n    electric = np.asarray(electric_field)\n    magnetic = np.asarray(magnetic_field)\n    if electric.shape != magnetic.shape:\n        raise ValueError(\"electric_field and magnetic_field must have matching shapes\")\n    if electric.ndim != 4 or electric.shape[-1] != 3:\n        raise ValueError(\"field arrays must have shape (nx, ny, nz, 3)\")\n\n    updated_electric = electric.astype(np.result_type(electric.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n    updated_magnetic = magnetic.astype(np.result_type(magnetic.dtype, np.complex128), copy=True)\n\n    # Direction sign: +1 for forward (+), -1 for backward (-)\n    direction_sign = 1.0 if compiled_source.direction == \"+\" else -1.0\n    injection_axis = compiled_source.injection_axis\n\n    # Get tangential axes\n    tang_axes = tuple(a for a in range(3) if a != injection_axis)\n    tang_axis_a, tang_axis_b = tang_axes\n\n    # Get the source amplitude at this time\n    amplitude = compiled_source.amplitude_at_time(time) * dt\n\n    # Get the direction and polarization vectors\n    dir_vec = compiled_source.dir_vector\n    pol_vec = compiled_source.pol_vector\n\n    # Normalize direction\n    dir_mag = math.sqrt(sum(d**2 for d in dir_vec))\n    if dir_mag > 0:\n        dir_hat = tuple(d / dir_mag for d in dir_vec)\n    else:\n        dir_hat = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0) if injection_axis == 2 else (0.0, 1.0, 0.0)\n\n    # Polarization direction\n    pol_hat = pol_vec\n\n    # Compute H direction as cross product of direction and polarization\n    h_dir_x = dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[2] - dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[1]\n    h_dir_y = dir_hat[2] * pol_hat[0] - dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[2]\n    h_dir_z = dir_hat[0] * pol_hat[1] - dir_hat[1] * pol_hat[0]\n    h_dir = (h_dir_x, h_dir_y, h_dir_z)\n\n    # For vacuum impedance in normalized units\n    z0_normalized = 1.0\n\n    # TFSF injection has two components:\n    # 1. Inject the incident field at the injection plane (forward face)\n    # 2. Apply the TF/SF boundary correction at all six faces\n\n    # For Phase 1, we implement the injection component only.\n    # The full TFSF boundary correction (subtracting incident field at the\n    # box edges) requires additional tracking of the incident field and\n    # is deferred to a later task that implements the full TFSF boundary stage.\n\n    # The injection plane is at one face of the TFSF box. For direction=\"+\",\n    # the injection plane is at the negative face of the box along the\n    # injection axis. For direction=\"-\", it's at the positive face.\n\n    # Get TFSF bounds indices\n    (imin, jmin, kmin), (imax, jmax, kmax) = compiled_source.tfsf_bounds_indices\n\n    # For each placement in the TFSF volume, inject the plane wave fields\n    # via equivalence principle (same as plane wave injection)\n    for placement, weight in zip(\n        compiled_source.placements,\n        compiled_source.placement_weights,\n        strict=True,\n    ):\n        # E field at this position (along polarization)\n        e_tang_a = pol_hat[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight\n        e_tang_b = pol_hat[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight\n\n        # H field at this position (perpendicular to both k and pol)\n        h_tang_a = h_dir[tang_axis_a] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n        h_tang_b = h_dir[tang_axis_b] * amplitude * weight / z0_normalized\n\n        # Apply via equivalence principle:\n        # J = n × H → contributes to E along tangential axes\n        # M = -n × E → contributes to H along tangential axes\n\n        if direction_sign > 0:\n            # Forward: H tangential contributes to E via J = n × H\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-h_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_electric[placement][tang_axis_b] += h_tang_a * direction_sign\n            # M = -n × E gives H update from E tangential\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_a] += (-e_tang_b) * direction_sign\n            updated_magnetic[placement][tang_axis_b] += e_tang_a * direction_sign\n        else:\n            # runtime-deferred error until mode-solver work lands.\n65\t- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` now lowers `ABCBoundary` into typed `ABCBoundaryIR` and records `abc_axes` / `abc_faces` on `BoundarySpecIR`; `ModeABCBoundary` remains a deferred public-model surface rather than a validated simulation feature.\n66\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/runtime.py` now compiles `Simulation` runtime controls into explicit `CompiledRuntimeControls` metadata with CFL-derived `dt`, Tidy3D-style `num_time_steps`, optional step-count override, shutoff policy, and carried `normalize_index`; `src/autofdtd/runtime/controls.py` evaluates those policies against integrated electric-field intensity without entangling them with the future Maxwell stepper.\n67\t- `src/autofdtd/diagnostics/runtime_logging.py` now provides `RuntimeProgressLogger`, immutable `RuntimeLogEvent` / `RuntimeExecutionLog` records, and explicit `RuntimeConvergenceEvidence` capture; `src/autofdtd/runtime/controls.py` now exposes `run_until_stop_with_logging()` as the logged execution seam for precomputed intensity histories.\n68\t- `src/autofdtd/sources/time.py` now provides Phase 1 `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` source-time profiles with finite parameter validation, analytic waveform evaluation, frequency-range helpers, and JSON-stable serialization through the shared tagged-model base.\n69\t- `src/autofdtd/sources/time.py` now also implements a supported `BroadbandPulse` Gaussian-subset wrapper and an interpolated `CustomSourceTime` with explicit `(time_samples, envelope_values)` storage using JSON-safe complex pairs.\n70\t- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` now lowers source-time profiles into typed `GaussianPulseIR` and `ContinuousWaveIR` payloads, and generic source component envelopes serialize nested `source_time` entries as typed IR dicts rather than opaque model payloads.\n71\t- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` now also lowers `BroadbandPulse` and `CustomSourceTime` into typed IR payloads carrying derived timing/frequency metadata plus serialized custom-envelope samples.\n72\t- `src/autofdtd/sources/current.py` now implements `UniformCurrentSource` with Tidy3D-aligned `center`, `size`, `polarization`, `source_time`, `interpolate`, `confine_to_bounds`, and `current_amplitude_definition` fields plus explicit `field_kind`, `component_axis`, `zero_size_axes`, `placement_kind`, and `support_bounds` semantics.\n73\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/sources.py`, `src/autofdtd/kernels/sources.py`, and `src/autofdtd/runtime/sources.py` now compile uniform current sources onto resolved primal-cell grids using overlap-or-interpolation axis placement, total-current normalization by transverse extent, and staged NumPy injection helpers for `(nx, ny, nz, 3)` electric or magnetic field buffers.\n74\t- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` now lowers `UniformCurrentSource` into typed `UniformCurrentSourceIR` payloads instead of only the generic source envelope, and `src/autofdtd/examples/current_sources.py` plus `tests/test_sources_current.py` exercise placement, IR lowering, compilation, and runtime injection end to end.\n75\t- `src/autofdtd/sources/beam.py` now implements `GaussianBeam` and `AstigmaticGaussianBeam` with planar injection geometry, direction/polarization vectors, waist parameters, angular spec support (`FixedInPlaneKSpec`/`FixedAngleSpec`), and equivalence-principle injection.\n76\t- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` lowers `GaussianBeam` into `GaussianBeamIR` and `AstigmaticGaussianBeam` into `AstigmaticGaussianBeamIR` with beam-specific fields (waist radii, waist distances, dir/pol vectors).\n77\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/sources.py` provides `compile_gaussian_beam` and `compile_astigmatic_gaussian_beam` with `CompiledGaussianBeam`/`CompiledAstigmaticGaussianBeam` dataclasses including Gaussian envelope weights and k-vector functions.\n78\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/sources.py` provides `inject_gaussian_beam` and `inject_astigmatic_gaussian_beam` using equivalence principle (J = n × H, M = -n × E) with spatially varying Gaussian envelope weights.\n79\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/sources.py` provides `build_gaussian_beam_runtime`, `apply_gaussian_beam_sources`, `build_astigmatic_gaussian_beam_runtime`, `apply_astigmatic_gaussian_beam_sources` for runtime integration.\n80\t- `tests/test_sources_beam.py` has 13 tests covering planar geometry validation, phase properties, direction vectors, waist validation, IR lowering, compilation, injection kernels, runtime integration, and angle support.\n81\t\n82\t## Monitor Facts (task-036)\n83\t\n84\t- `src/autofdtd/monitors/models.py` now provides typed monitor models (`FieldMonitor`, `FluxMonitor`, `ModeMonitor`, etc.) with name normalization, unique-name validation, and interval/start semantics.\n85\t- `src/autofdtd/monitors/__init__.py` exports the full monitor public API including `SimulationData`, `MonitorData`, `FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData`, `PermittivityData`, and `monitor_model_from_value`.\n86\t- `SimulationData` provides Tidy3D-style named access: dictionary-style `sim_data[\"monitor_name\"]` and attribute-style `sim_data.monitor_name` for registered monitor data.\n87\t- `SimulationData` uses a `monitor_store` field (excluded from serialization) to hold runtime data, with registration methods (`register_field_monitor`, `register_flux_monitor`, etc.) that enforce unique-name constraints.\n88\t- `src/autofdtd/ir/models.py` now has typed monitor IR models: `FieldMonitorIR`, `FieldTimeMonitorIR`, `AuxFieldTimeMonitorIR`, `FluxMonitorIR`, `FluxTimeMonitorIR`, `ModeMonitorIR`, `ModeSolverMonitorIR`, `MediumMonitorIR`, `PermittivityMonitorIR`, `FieldProjectionAngleMonitorIR`, `FieldProjectionCartesianMonitorIR`, `FieldProjectionKSpaceMonitorIR`, `DiffractionMonitorIR`, `DirectivityMonitorIR`, `GaussianOverlapMonitorIR`, `AstigmaticGaussianOverlapMonitorIR`, `SurfaceFieldMonitorIR`, `SurfaceFieldTimeMonitorIR`.\n89\t- `monitor_to_ir()` function in `ir/models.py` lowers concrete monitor models to typed IR payloads, falling back to generic `ComponentIR` for unknown or incomplete monitor types.\n90\t- `SimulationIR.monitors` field is typed as `tuple[MonitorComponentIR, ...]` where `MonitorComponentIR` is the union of all typed monitor IRs plus `ComponentIR`.\n91\t- `src/autofdtd/core/validation.py` now has `_normalize_monitor()` and `_PHASE1_MONITOR_TYPES` frozenset for monitor validation; `normalize_component()` dispatches to `_normalize_monitor()` for `context=\"monitor\"`.\n92\t- `src/autofdtd/planning.py` now has `ModeSpec` and `MediumMonitor` added to the feature matrix as IMPLEMENT features since they are used by `ModeMonitor` and `ModeSolverMonitor`.\n93\t- Monitor data types (`FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData`, `PermittivityData`) store complex values as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n94\t\n95\t## Field Monitor Facts (task-037)\n96\t\n97\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFieldMonitor`, `FieldMonitorState`, `compile_field_monitor()` for field monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n98\t- `CompiledFieldMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, fields, interval, start) plus discrete placements and weights derived from the resolved grid.\n99\t- `FieldMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass that stores runtime recording state; time-domain monitors use `time_series` dict and `time_stamps` list, while frequency-domain monitors use `dft_data` dict and `dft_count` counter.\n100\t- Frozen dataclass field updates use `object.__setattr__` and `_set_dft_count` / `dft_count_value` accessors to bypass immutability for accumulating state.\n101\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `record_field_time_domain()`, `record_field_frequency_domain()`, `sample_field_at_points()`, and `accumulate_flux()` for field recording kernels.\n102\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_field_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_fields()`, and `extract_monitor_data()` for runtime field monitor orchestration.\n103\t- `monitor_to_ir()` is exported from `autofdtd.ir` for IR lowering of all monitor types.\n104\t- Field monitor compilation uses `_compile_monitor_axis_placement()` which derives placements from monitor center/size against grid boundaries.\n105\t- FieldMonitor with explicit `freqs` tuple is treated as frequency-domain (`is_frequency_domain=True`), otherwise it is not (even if monitor_type is FieldMonitor).\n106\t- Time-domain monitors record at every `interval` steps starting from `start` step; `record_monitor_fields()` handles the step filtering.\n107\t- `tests/test_monitors_field.py` has 23 tests covering compilation, state initialization, time-domain recording, frequency-domain recording, flux accumulation, field sampling, and IR lowering.\n108\t\n109\t## Flux Monitor Facts (task-039)\n110\t\n111\t- `src/autofdtd/compiler/monitors.py` now provides `CompiledFluxMonitor`, `FluxMonitorState`, `compile_flux_monitor()` for flux monitor compilation onto resolved grids.\n112\t- `_infer_flux_normal_axis()` derives the normal axis from monitor size (the dimension with size=0), raising ValueError if no dimension is zero.\n113\t- `CompiledFluxMonitor` carries monitor metadata (name, type, center, size, normal_axis, direction, placements, interval, start) plus discrete surface placements and weights.\n114\t- `FluxMonitorState` is a frozen dataclass for flux recording; time-domain uses `flux_series` list and `time_stamps` list, frequency-domain uses `dft_flux` array and `dft_count` counter.\n115\t- `FluxMonitorState.record_flux_time_domain()` and `accumulate_flux_dft()` compute Poynting vector flux through the surface using `accumulate_flux()` kernel.\n116\t- `accumulate_flux()` computes the surface integral of the Poynting vector S = E × H; for axis=0 (x-normal): S_x = E_y*H_z - E_z*H_y; axis=1 (y-normal): S_y = E_z*H_x - E_x*H_z; axis=2 (z-normal): S_z = E_x*H_y - E_y*H_x.\n117\t- `FluxData` stores flux values; for time-domain (FluxTimeMonitor) `flux` is `tuple[float, ...]`, for frequency-domain (FluxMonitor with freqs) `flux` is `tuple[tuple[float, float], ...]` as (real, imag) complex tuples.\n118\t- `src/autofdtd/runtime/monitors.py` provides `build_flux_monitor_runtime()`, `record_monitor_flux()`, and `extract_flux_monitor_data()` for runtime flux monitor orchestration.\n119\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/monitors.py` provides `flux_monitor_kernel_metadata()`, `record_flux_time_domain()`, and `record_flux_frequency_domain()` for flux recording kernels.\n120\t- `FluxMonitor` with `freqs` tuple is frequency-domain; `FluxTimeMonitor` is always time-domain.\n121\t- `tests/test_monitors_flux.py` has 39 tests covering normal axis inference, model construction, compilation, time-domain and frequency-domain recording, flux integration (Poynting vector), IR lowering, and energy-flow validation.\n122\t\n123\t## Decisions\n124\t\n125\t- Reserve subsystem namespaces now: `api`, `core`, `geometry`, `materials`, `boundaries`, `sources`, `monitors`, `grid`, `modes`, `ir`, `compiler`, `runtime`, `kernels`, `diagnostics`, `examples`.\n126\t- Use `hatchling` with a `src/` layout and ship a small CLI (`autofdtd-feature-matrix`) so the scaffold has a concrete install/runtime smoke target before solver code exists.\n127\t- Keep this task focused on packaging, docs, and planning metadata; full tagged models remain for `task-002` and `task-003`.\n128\t- Model ordered overlap explicitly through `Scene.structures_in_resolution_order()`, sorting by resolved priority and then insertion order so later structures still win ties during sequential materialization.\n129\t- Keep the initial container layer serialization-light: `to_payload()`, `to_json_text()`, and `write_json()` are enough for snapshots and handoff, while `task-005` can generalize the shared model base.\n130\t- Use a generic family-tagged component envelope in the IR so Phase 1 can package realistic simulations before every feature family has concrete typed IR classes; later tasks can specialize payloads without changing bundle shape.\n131\t- Bundle packaging is directory-based: `manifest.json` points at a hashed `simulation.json` entrypoint, avoiding self-referential manifest hashing while staying suitable for remote upload or local caching.\n132\t- Core validation is checklist-driven: implemented feature tags pass, deferred or rejected tags fail early with explicit errors, and known primitive geometry payloads validate only when enough fields are present so placeholder tagged dicts can survive until their concrete family tasks land.\n133\t- Shared model behavior should live in `autofdtd.core.models`; public API and IR classes should subclass it rather than duplicating serialization or copy helpers in subsystem-local base classes.\n134\t- Primitive geometry stays axis-aligned in Phase 1 public models; scene-compilation transforms are still explicit so later `Transformed` and grouped-geometry tasks can lower onto the same contract instead of rewriting primitive handling.\n135\t- Structure precedence is now inspectable with `Scene.resolve_structure_at_point()`, which mirrors materialization order by applying primitive containment checks in resolved-priority order.\n136\t- Keep `GeometryArray` as a public wrapper only; repeated placements lower into ordinary grouped and transformed instances so runtime/compiler code can treat arrays as pre-expanded ordered geometry.\n137\t- Phase 1 `PolySlab` scope is intentionally limited to constant-cross-section extrusions of simple polygons on principal axes; non-zero `dilation` or `sidewall_angle`, self-intersections, and curved or boolean polygon features must fail explicitly until later geometry tasks land.\n138\t- Keep the feature ledger conservative for clipping: `ClipOperation` remains a deferred family in `planning.py`, but geometry validation explicitly allows the implemented subset and rejects unsupported boolean ops with clear errors.\n139\t- Phase 1 transform wrappers are rigid only; free-form affine matrices and exact boolean meshing remain out of scope even though the public model now supports grouped and transformed composition.\n140\t- Keep the top-level simulation grid surface strict: `grid_spec` must be a `GridSpec`, `AutoGrid` requires `GridSpec.wavelength`, and `QuasiUniformGrid` remains an explicit deferred error.\n141\t- Phase 1 AutoGrid is deliberately narrow: derive a vacuum baseline from wavelength and domain size, discretize piecewise with a simple `max_scale` grading pass, and limit refinement inputs to `LayerRefinementSpec` body and bound hints rather than full geometry-driven meshing.\n142\t- Keep Phase 1 subpixel support narrow and explicit: dielectric interfaces may use `PolarizedAveraging` or `Staircasing`, while metal, PEC, PMC, and lossy-metal interfaces stay staircasing-only until anisotropic media and conformal policies land.\n143\t- Treat PEC and PMC as explicit constitutive clamp modes in coefficient compilation and runtime helpers instead of pretending they are extremely lossy ordinary media.\n144\t- Phase 1 `PoleResidue` keeps complex numbers JSON-safe as `(real, imag)` tuples in the public API and IR so serialization stays stable without inventing ad hoc string encoding.\n145\t- Phase 1 PoleResidue runtime support is electric-only and uses one complex polarization accumulator per pole; magnetic dispersion remains out of scope until later material families require it.\n146\t- Phase 1 `Sellmeier` support is intentionally passive-only and homogeneous-only: `B_i >= 0`, `C_i >= 0`, zero-`C_i` terms fold into `eps_inf`, and all timestep execution reuses the existing electric-only `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` path instead of creating a new auxiliary-state layout.\n147\t- Phase 1 `Lorentz` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `damping >= 0`, `frequency^2 != damping^2`), `Drude` is limited to damped poles (`damping > 0`) so it can lower cleanly onto the current pole-residue path, and `Debye` is passive-only (`delta_eps >= 0`, `tau > 0`).\n148\t- The current exact-hold pole-residue recurrence path can overflow for sufficiently stiff poles relative to `dt`; material compilation now raises an explicit error instead of emitting unusable coefficients, which is especially relevant for very small-`tau` Debye fits.\n149\t- Phase 1 anisotropy stops at diagonal tensor media: `AnisotropicMedium` is implemented and IR-lowered, `FullyAnisotropicMedium` remains an explicit deferred error for runtime compilation, and `Medium2D` remains a deferred direct-simulation feature with offline conversion helpers only.\n150\t- Advanced material policy is explicit rather than silent: `LossyMetalMedium` and perturbation media are parseable defer buckets with manual fallback helpers, while sampled `Custom*` media are parsed or bucketed only to produce clear reject errors.\n151\t- Phase 1 boundary runtime support now covers `Periodic`, `BlochBoundary`, `PECBoundary`, `PMCBoundary`, and baseline `PML`; `StablePML`, `Absorber`, and ABC-family entries remain outside the runtime compiler until later boundary tasks land.\n152\t- Conductor boundaries are represented as reflection-sign transforms rather than special-casing them inside the future timestep loop: PEC uses odd tangential electric and odd normal magnetic parity, while PMC uses the opposite parity assignment.\n153\t- Keep Phase 1 PML deliberately narrow: use Tidy3D-shaped `num_layers + parameters + extrude_structures` public models, compile one profile per boundary face, and treat the first runnable backend as a staged damping layer with persistent face memory rather than a full split-field CPML implementation.\n154\t- Keep `StablePML` as a preset-driven extension of the baseline staged PML backend in Phase 1 rather than a separate field-splitting formulation; runtime differentiation is through compiled profile metadata and defaults, not a distinct kernel family.\n155\t- Keep `Absorber` practical and explicit: Phase 1 uses a conductivity-ramp absorbing subset with no auxiliary memory contribution and a PEC-like outer-wall reflection policy, trading fidelity for a clear stable fallback when baseline PML is unsuitable.\n156\t- Keep `ABCBoundary` practical and narrow: Phase 1 implements only the explicit-parameter first-order subset, and automatic medium inference plus mode-based ABC stay deferred until scene-material sampling and mode-solver tasks are available.\n157\t- Keep runtime convergence explicit and narrow: Phase 1 shutoff is the Tidy3D-style ratio of instantaneous integrated electric-field intensity to its historical maximum, checked on a configurable step interval; material-aware CFL relaxation and richer convergence heuristics remain deferred.\n158\t- Keep runtime logging separate from benchmark metrics: Phase 1 emits deterministic progress records at step 0, configurable progress intervals, and final stop, while every shutoff check still lands in convergence evidence even when progress logging is downsampled.\n159\t- Keep Phase 1 source-time scope narrow and explicit: implement `GaussianPulse` and `ContinuousWave` as standalone immutable models first, preserve Tidy3D-style `freq0`/`fwidth`/`offset` semantics plus `remove_dc_component` on Gaussian pulses, and defer broader source-time families to their own tasks instead of hiding them behind loose dict payloads.\n160\t- Phase 1 `BroadbandPulse` is a clearly delimited analytic subset: represent it as a Gaussian-modulated carrier derived from `(freq_range, minimum_amplitude)` rather than trying to reproduce Tidy3D's licensed extension internals, and keep `CustomSourceTime` as explicit sample interpolation with nearest-value clamping outside the provided range.\n161\t- Phase 1 `UniformCurrentSource` uses primal-cell placement metadata rather than hidden source snapping: zero-size axes interpolate between neighboring cell centers when enabled, finite extents either select center-in-bounds cells or overlap-fraction weights when `confine_to_bounds=True`, and `\"total\"` amplitude normalization divides by the local transverse extent implied by the source geometry and resolved grid.\n162\t\n163\t## Task Handoffs\n164\t\n165\t- `task-003` should add versioned IR models under `autofdtd.ir` without coupling them to the public API module layout; it can treat `Simulation.to_payload()` as an interim handoff format, not the final IR contract.\n166\t- Later geometry, material, source, and monitor tasks can plug concrete tagged models into the existing container fields without changing the ordered-container semantics.\n167\t- `task-004` and `task-005` can reuse `ComponentIR.payload` plus the shared IR schema version constants instead of inventing another transport shape.\n168\t- Later geometry, source, monitor, boundary, and grid tasks should route new object models through `autofdtd.core.validation.normalize_component()` or extend its equivalents instead of scattering policy checks across each family.\n169\t- Later compiler work can rely on wrapper IR bounds as conservative planning envelopes; exact voxelization or boolean rasterization still belongs in scene compilation rather than the public geometry layer.\n170\t- Later compiler work can consume `StructureIR.source_index` plus `StructureIR.precedence_rank` directly when building ordered region materialization plans or conflict diagnostics.\n171\t- Later grid/compiler tasks should treat `LayerRefinementSpecIR` as planning metadata only; corner finding, snapping-point localization, and structure-derived meshing still need a separate scene-aware meshing pass.\n172\t- Material and compiler tasks should consume `SubpixelSpecIR.averaging_targets` and `requires_anisotropic_materialization` directly instead of re-deriving which interface families need tensor-valued effective media.\n173\t- Later runtime and chunk-planning tasks can treat `IsotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus `ConstitutiveMode` as the stable baseline constitutive contract before dispersive and anisotropic state is added.\n174\t- Later Lorentz, Drude, Debye, and Sellmeier tasks should lower onto the `PoleResidueMaterialCoefficients` auxiliary-state contract whenever the family can be reduced to pole-residue terms instead of creating parallel runtime state layouts.\n175\t- Later subpixel materialization work can target `AnisotropicMaterialCoefficients` plus the diagonal anisotropic kernel path instead of inventing a separate tensor-material runtime for effective-media smoothing.\n176\t- Later absorbing-boundary tasks should extend `CompiledBoundarySpec` and the boundary ghost-update stage rather than reworking the public `Boundary` or `BoundarySpec` container semantics; Bloch, symmetry, and baseline PML metadata now already live on that runtime contract.\n177\t- `task-022` should either preserve the `PMLParams` coefficient-shape contract for `StablePML` / `Absorber` or add a clearly separate compiled-face payload; `allocate_pml_boundary_state()` and `apply_boundary_stages()` are the current extension seams.\n178\t- Later ABC-boundary work should treat the current `pml_faces` metadata on `CompiledBoundarySpec` as the legacy absorbing-face alias and prefer the newer `stable_pml_faces` / `absorber_faces` tags when family-specific behavior matters.\n179\t- Later mode-ABC work should extend the new `abc_faces` / staged-ABC seam rather than folding mode-aware behavior into the generic halo transform path; the current deferral is specifically about mode-solver dependency, not about the ABC stage layout itself.\n180\t- Later execution-loop tasks should treat `CompiledRuntimeControls` and `RuntimeController` as the stable seam for stop reasons, early-stop evidence, and step-budget bookkeeping rather than burying convergence logic inside kernels or chunk schedulers.\n181\t- Later execution-loop tasks should route full Maxwell stepping through `RuntimeProgressLogger` / `RuntimeExecutionLog` instead of inventing a second progress format; benchmark tasks can layer throughput and JIT timing on top of the existing log bundle rather than mixing those concerns into debugging output.\n182\t- Later source-family tasks should reuse `source_time_model_from_value()` and `source_time_to_ir()` as the normalization and lowering seam for nested `source_time` fields instead of reimplementing waveform parsing inside each source model.\n183\t- Later source-time expansions should preserve the current JSON-safe complex-pair representation for custom envelopes so package serialization and IR transport stay stable even if dataset-backed APIs are added later.\n184\t- Later `PointDipole` and `CustomCurrentSource` work should reuse `CompiledAxisPlacement` / `CompiledUniformCurrentSource` conventions where possible, extending them for Yee-staggered component placement or dataset-driven amplitudes instead of inventing a separate source-placement runtime contract.\n185\t\n186\t## Chunk Contract Task Handoffs (task-047)\n187\t\n188\t- `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md` defines the full chunk contract including `ChunkSpec`, `FaceHalo`, `ChunkLayout`, per-chunk PML/ABC state, and device/rank placement.\n189\t- task-048 (Warp timestepping kernel) should consume `ChunkLayout` and `ChunkSpec` to allocate per-chunk field arrays and implement chunk-local `electric_update_kernel` / `magnetic_update_kernel`.\n190\t- task-049 (runtime executor) should consume `CompiledSimulation` + `ChunkLayout` to build per-chunk field state and implement `run_chunked_simulation()` with stage order: boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → monitor_collection → pml_stage → convergence_check.\n191\t- task-050 (MPI halo exchange) should extend `ChunkLayout.rank_assignment` to multiple ranks and implement `mpi_halo_exchange()` using `exchange_plan`.\n192\t- task-051 (CFL / chunk balancing) should use `ChunkSpec.local_grid_shape` and per-axis `cell_sizes` to compute per-chunk `min_step`; use global minimum if varying.\n193\t- task-053 (monitor accumulation kernels) should use `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` for owning-chunk routing and implement gather/reduce for multi-chunk monitors (flux surfaces, mode overlaps).\n194\t- `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec` (`compiler/boundaries.py`) is the concrete refactoring point — replace with per-face `FaceHalo` from `ChunkLayout`.\n195\t\n196\t## Mode Solver Facts (task-031)\n197\t\n198\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/__init__.py` now exports the public mode-solver API including `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverConfig`, `ModeSolverCrossSection`, `BoundaryCondition`, `ModeSolution`, `solve_modes`, `assemble_mode_matrix`, `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d`, and full IR lowering.\n199\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/models.py` provides `ModeSpec`, `ModeSolverCrossSection` (with normal_axis and boundary tuple), `BoundaryCondition` (dirichlet/neumann/bloch), and `ModeSolution` with field components stored as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n200\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/solver.py` implements `solve_modes` using a 2-component curl-curl FDTD eigenvalue formulation: `A·[Hx;Hy] = β²·[Hx;Hy]` with sparse matrix assembly and scipy `eigs` for the generalized eigenvalue problem. The formulation uses the VectorModesolver.jl paper (IEEE) as the reference for coefficient derivations.\n201\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/epsilon.py` provides `sample_scene_epsilon_tensor_2d` which samples a Scene's permittivity onto a rectilinear cross-sectional grid, returning an `EpsilonCallback` compatible with the mode solver.\n202\t- `src/autofdtd/modes/ir.py` provides typed IR models `ModeSpecIR`, `BoundaryConditionIR`, `ModeSolverCrossSectionIR`, `ModeSolverConfigIR`, `ModeSolutionIR` with lowering functions `mode_spec_to_ir`, `boundary_condition_to_ir`, `cross_section_to_ir`, `mode_solver_config_to_ir`, `mode_solution_to_ir`.\n203\t- The solver requires scipy and will skip (not fail) when scipy is unavailable. The assembly and solve use double precision.\n204\t- Phase 1 mode solver scope: works with general anisotropic epsilon tensors, supports Dirichlet/Neumann/Bloch BCs, and produces ModeSolution objects with field components and power normalization.\n205\t- Known limitations: the current curl-curl formulation produces neff ≈ n_clad for weakly guided modes in small domains due to discretization scaling (1/h² vs k² ratios); the formulation needs careful physical parameter selection for truly guided-mode separation. This is a known FDTD eigenvalue challenge and is acceptable for Phase 1 as the subsystem interface and architecture are Warp-ready.\n206\t\n207\t## Monitor Task Handoffs (task-036)\n208\t\n209\t- Later monitor tasks (task-037 for field monitors, task-038 for medium monitors, task-039 for flux monitors, etc.) should reuse `monitor_to_ir()` for IR lowering and `monitor_model_from_value()` for validation/normalization instead of reimplementing these patterns.\n210\t- Later monitor tasks should extend `SimulationData` with specific registration methods (like `register_field_monitor`) and use `FieldData`, `FluxData`, `ModeData` etc. as the data container types.\n211\t- Monitor IR lowering is designed to be extensible: `monitor_to_ir()` falls back to generic `ComponentIR` for unknown types, so later tasks can add new monitor IR types without modifying the lowering contract.\n212\t- The `SimulationData.monitor_store` field is excluded from serialization by design - runtime monitor data is populated during simulation execution, not serialized with the simulation metadata.\n213\t- `Phase1_MONITOR_TYPES` in `core/validation.py` and `_MONITOR_CONSTRUCTORS` in `monitors/models.py` are the canonical lists of implemented monitor types; add new types to both when implementing new monitor families.\n214\t\n215\t## Task Handoffs (continued)\n216\t\n217\t## Medium Monitor Facts (task-038)\n218\t\n219\t- `MediumMonitorData` model in `monitors/models.py` stores diagonal components (eps_xx, eps_yy, eps_zz, mu_xx, mu_yy, mu_zz) as `(real, imag)` tuples for JSON stability.\n220\t- `PermittivityMonitor` already had `PermittivityData` with real-valued eps components; `MediumMonitorData` extends with complex-valued permittivity + permeability.\n221\t- orchestrates the full compilation pipeline from public API → IR → compiled runtime artifacts.\n258\t- `CompiledSimulation` is a frozen dataclass (not Pydantic model) that holds all compiled artifacts: field allocation is deferred to the geometry discretization task (task-046).\n265\t- `simulation_to_execution_package()` combines compilation with `ExecutionPackageIR` bundling for transportable simulation bundles.\n266\t- Pipeline stages: (1) grid resolution, (2) IR lowering via `simulation_to_ir()`, (3) runtime controls compilation, (4) scene materialization, (5) source planning, (6) monitor planning, (7) boundary planning.\n267\t\n268\t## Chunk Architecture Facts (task-047)\n269\t\n270\t- Chunk is the architectural unit for field storage, field update, and parallelism; rank or device is placement, not computation.\n271\t- Current Phase 1 architecture is **monolithic**: `CompiledSimulation` treats the entire grid as one chunk with global `halo_depth=1` in `CompiledBoundarySpec`.\n272\t- `ChunkSpec` (in `phase1/architecture/chunk-contract.md`) is the planning metadata for one chunk: `chunk_index`, `global_bounds`, `interior_bounds`, `owned_bounds`, `local_grid_shape`, `face_halos`, `source_indices`, `monitor_indices`.\n273\t- `FaceHalo` describes per-face halo metadata: `depth`, `neighbor_chunk_index`, `exchange_kind`, `phase_factor`, `electric_signs`, `magnetic_signs`, `pml_profile`, `abc_coefficients`.\n274\t- `ChunkLayout` is the global decomposition plan: `num_chunks`, `chunks`, `device_assignment` (chunk_index → CUDA device), `rank_assignment` (chunk_index → MPI rank), `exchange_plan`.\n275\t- Halo exchange is **stage-specific** and **component-family-specific**: `boundary_exchange` before E update, `boundary_exchange` before H update, `pml_stage` after H update.\n276\t- Halo depth is **per-face**, not global: periodic/Bloch=1 cell, PEC/PMC=1 cell, PML=variable (num_layers), ABC=1 cell, symmetry=1 cell, interior=1 cell.\n277\t- Symmetry reduction produces `ChunkSpec.is_reduced` and `ChunkSpec.symmetry_multiplicity`; symmetry transform is a logical address transform, not a data-layout change.\n278\t- Periodic/Bloch remapping: `ChunkLayout.exchange_plan` connects plus face of last chunk to minus face of first chunk; Bloch exchange includes `phase_factor` from `BlochBoundaryIR`.\n279\t- PML/ABC state must be **per-chunk per-face**: `ChunkPMLFaceState` (sigma/kappa/alpha profiles + memory array) and `ChunkABCFaceState` (previous_boundary, previous_adjacent arrays).\n280\t- `ChunkSpec.source_indices` and `ChunkSpec.monitor_indices` route source injection and monitor recording to owning chunks.\n281\t- For CUDA: field arrays are `wp.array` objects per chunk on target device; Warp kernel launches use `chunk.local_grid_shape` as launch dimensions.\n282\t- `halo_depth=1` global constant in `CompiledBoundarySpec` must be replaced with per-face `FaceHalo.depth` — this is a concrete refactoring point.\n283\t- Key deferred items: MPI halo exchange (task-050+), dynamic load balancing (Meep-style chunk_balancer), overlapped/systolic execution (task-052+), chunk-local CFL.\n284\t\n285\t## Warp Backend Conventions Facts (task-048)\n286\t\n287\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/backend.py` now provides the foundational Warp backend conventions: device management, persistent array tags, module stability tracking, complex field policy, and profiling hooks.\n288\t- `src/autofdtd/kernels/steps.py` now provides the core Maxwell timestepping kernel definitions: `electric_update_3d`, `magnetic_update_3d`, `MaxwellStepKernelSpec`, `allocate_maxwell_arrays()`, `step_maxwell()`, and `step_kernel_metadata()`.\n289\t- `WARP_AVAILABLE = warp is not None` is the canonical availability flag; each kernel module has its own `WARP_AVAILABLE` derived from the optional `import warp as wp`.\n290\t- `allocate_field_array()` allocates `(Nx, Ny, Nz, 3)` vector fields with `FieldArrayTag` attached; `allocate_coefficient_array()` allocates `(Nx, Ny, Nz)` coefficient fields with `CoefficientArrayTag`; `allocate_auxiliary_array()` allocates `(num_poles, Nx, Ny, Nz)` polarization arrays with `AuxiliaryArrayTag`.\n291\t- `ComplexFieldPolicy` determines when complex dtype is required: `requires_complex = force_complex or has_bloch_axis`. Bloch boundaries force complex fields even when `bloch_vec == 0`.\n292\t- `ModuleStability` tracks stable modules: `mark_module_stable(name)` and `is_module_stable(name)` prevent JIT churn from unstable kernels.\n293\t- Capture-safe stepping: all timestep-varying values (dt, time, step_index) are passed as explicit kernel arguments; no closure captures in kernel definitions.\n294\t- Profiling hooks: `WarpTimer(name)` context manager (GPU-side timing via ScopedTimer when available, CPU fallback otherwise), `nvtx_range(name, color)` for NVTX annotation, `step_metrics()` for structured Gcells/s metrics.\n295\t- `step_kernel_metadata()` returns update stage order: `boundary_exchange → electric_update → source_injection → boundary_exchange → magnetic_update → pml_stage → monitor_collection → convergence_check`.\n296\t- `curl_component_mapping` documents the Yee lattice curl formula for each field component: Ex uses (Hz, dy, Hy, dz), etc.\n297\t- `tests/test_kernels_backend.py` has 27 tests covering availability detection, device management, array tags, allocation helpers, complex field policy, module stability, and profiling hooks.\n298\t- Warp is not installed in the current environment; all allocation helpers fall back to NumPy when Warp is unavailable.\n299\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"3aeecfc1-a319-4311-bc41-2135839dedf2","uuid":"67f20caf-b63c-49ad-9ddb-fcb7bf387dba","timestamp":"2026-04-07T10:28:06.421Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/workspace/autofdtd/phase1/working-memory.md","content":"# Phase 1 Maxwell Solver Loop Working Memory\n\nUse this file for cumulative findings, reusable facts, decisions, and cross-task notes.\n\nKeep it concise.
